| `status.readyReplicas` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | Replicas tracks the number of ready vs desired pods |
| `status.replicas` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | Replicas is the current number of running inference pods |
| `status.schedulingMessage` | string |  |  |  | SchedulingMessage provides details about scheduling issues |
| `status.schedulingStatus` | string |  |  |  | SchedulingStatus indicates why pods cannot be scheduled (e.g., "InsufficientGPU") |
| `status.selector` | string |  |  |  | Selector is the label selector for the pods this service owns. It backs the /scale subresource's selectorpath; an HPA or KEDA target rejects an empty selector. |
| `status.waitingFor` | string |  |  |  | WaitingFor describes the resource constraint (e.g., "nvidia.com/gpu: 1") |

### LoRAAdapter

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `LoRAAdapter` |
| API versions | `inference.llmkube.dev/v1alpha1` |
| Scope | Namespaced |
| Plural | `loraadapters` |
| Short names | `lora` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/inference.llmkube.dev_loraadapters.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/inference.llmkube.dev_loraadapters.yaml) |

#### LoRAAdapter printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |

| Service | `string` | `.spec.inferenceServiceRef.name` | 0 |  |
| Adapter | `string` | `.spec.name` | 0 |  |
| Loaded | `string` | `.status.conditions[?(@.type=="Loaded")].status` | 0 |  |

#### LoRAAdapter spec

spec defines the desired state of LoRAAdapter

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.inferenceServiceRef` | object | yes |  |  | InferenceServiceRef names the InferenceService this adapter should be loaded into. The reconciler resolves it; if it points at a non-sglang runtime, Available=False with reason RuntimeMismatch. |
| `spec.inferenceServiceRef.name` | string | yes |  |  | Name is the name of the target InferenceService. Must exist in Namespace (or be omitted to default to this resource's namespace). |
| `spec.inferenceServiceRef.namespace` | string |  |  |  | Namespace is the namespace of the target InferenceService. When omitted, defaults to the LoRAAdapter's namespace. Cross-namespace references are allowed; the controller only requires that the operator has RBAC to GET the referenced InferenceService. |
| `spec.name` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$`; min length 1; max length 63 | Name is the SGLang-side adapter handle. Operators quote this in inference requests to pick the adapter. Must be unique within the target InferenceService's loaded set; the SGLang HTTP API rejects duplicates with 409. |
| `spec.path` | string | yes |  | min length 1 | Path is the path on disk inside the SGLang container where the adapter weights are mounted. SGLang reads from this path when /load_lora_adapter is invoked, so it must be reachable from the inference pod (typically via a PVC exposed in InferenceService.spec.extraVolumes) at the path the operator chose. The controller does not auto-mount. |

#### LoRAAdapter status

status defines the observed state of LoRAAdapter

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | conditions represent the current state of the LoRAAdapter resource. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.lastLoadedAt` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastLoadedAt is when the adapter was last successfully loaded into SGLang. Re-loads (after a SGLang pod restart, for example) update this field. |
| `status.loadedPath` | string |  |  |  | LoadedPath is the path SGLang has accepted for this adapter. Set after a successful POST /load_lora_adapter response. Empty before the first successful load. |

### MetalNode

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `MetalNode` |

| Scope | Namespaced |
| Plural | `metalnodes` |
| Short names | `mn` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/inference.llmkube.dev_metalnodes.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/inference.llmkube.dev_metalnodes.yaml) |

#### MetalNode printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Host IP | `string` | `.status.hostIP` | 0 |  |
| Memory | `string` | `.status.capacity.memory` | 0 |  |
| Chip | `string` | `.status.capacity.chip` | 0 |  |
| Reachable | `string` | `.status.conditions[?(@.type=="Reachable")].status` | 0 |  |
| Version | `string` | `.status.agentVersion` | 0 |  |

#### MetalNode spec

MetalNodeSpec is written by the cluster admin (llmkube metal token create). The agent has no write access to it.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.hostIP` | string |  |  | max length 45 | HostIP is an address the admin knows reaches this Mac. It is tried before the agent's own candidates and is still verified by the dial-back; it is never trusted on its own. The controller validates it (Reachable=False, reason InvalidSpec, when it is not an IP address) rather than a CEL rule, whose isIP() would need Kubernetes 1.31. |
| `spec.leaseName` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*$`; min length 1; max length 253 | LeaseName is the coordination.k8s.io Lease, in this namespace, that the agent renews as its heartbeat and annotates with its report. |
| `spec.serviceAccountName` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*$`; min length 1; max length 253 | ServiceAccountName is the ServiceAccount the Mac's agent authenticates as. Deleting that ServiceAccount revokes every token the agent holds. |
| `spec.verifier` | object |  |  |  | Verifier places the dial-back verifier pod. Point it at the nodes the relay pods for this Mac run on, so the check tests the network path the relay will use. |
| `spec.verifier.nodeSelector` | map of string |  |  |  | NodeSelector is copied to the verifier pod. |
| `spec.verifier.tolerations` | array of object |  |  |  | Tolerations are copied to the verifier pod. |
| `spec.verifier.tolerations[].effect` | string |  |  |  | Effect indicates the taint effect to match. Empty means match all taint effects. When specified, allowed values are NoSchedule, PreferNoSchedule and NoExecute. |
| `spec.verifier.tolerations[].key` | string |  |  |  | Key is the taint key that the toleration applies to. Empty means match all taint keys. If the key is empty, operator must be Exists; this combination means to match all values and all keys. |
| `spec.verifier.tolerations[].operator` | string |  |  |  | Operator represents a key's relationship to the value. Valid operators are Exists, Equal, Lt, and Gt. Defaults to Equal. Exists is equivalent to wildcard for value, so that a pod can tolerate all taints of a particular category. Lt and Gt perform numeric comparisons (requires feature gate TaintTolerationComparisonOperators). |
| `spec.verifier.tolerations[].tolerationSeconds` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | TolerationSeconds represents the period of time the toleration (which must be of effect NoExecute, otherwise this field is ignored) tolerates the taint. By default, it is not set, which means tolerate the taint forever (do not evict). Zero and negative values will be treated as 0 (evict immediately) by the system. |
| `spec.verifier.tolerations[].value` | string |  |  |  | Value is the taint value the toleration matches to. If the operator is Exists, the value should be empty, otherwise just a regular string. |

#### MetalNode status

MetalNodeStatus is written only by the controller.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.agentVersion` | string |  |  |  | AgentVersion is the version the agent reports. |
| `status.candidates` | array of object |  |  | max items 17 | Candidates are the addresses tried, in preference order, with the last dial-back result for each. |
| `status.candidates[].address` | string | yes |  |  | Address is an IP address. |
| `status.candidates[].checkedAt` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | CheckedAt is when the verifier finished the check that produced Result. |
| `status.candidates[].result` | string | yes |  | one of `"Pending"`, `"Reachable"`, `"Timeout"`, `"Refused"`, `"PinMismatch"`, `"CertificateMismatch"`, `"NotAllowed"`, `"Error"` | Result is the dial-back outcome. |
| `status.candidates[].source` | string |  |  |  | Source says where the address came from: override (spec.hostIP or the agent's `--host-ip`), tailnet, lan or docker-host. |
| `status.capacity` | object |  |  |  | Capacity is the hardware the agent reports. |
| `status.capacity.chip` | string |  |  |  | Chip is the CPU brand string on macOS (for example "Apple M5 Max"), and the device-tree model or the cpuinfo model name on Linux. |
| `status.capacity.gpuCores` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | GPUCores is the GPU core count. Reported on macOS only. |
| `status.capacity.memory` | string |  |  |  | Memory is MemoryBytes as a quantity (for example 128Gi), for display. |
| `status.capacity.memoryBytes` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | MemoryBytes is the host's memory in bytes: unified memory on a Mac, RAM capped by the agent's cgroup limit on Linux. |
| `status.capacity.metalVersion` | string |  |  |  | MetalVersion is the Metal family the GPU supports (for example "Metal 4"). Reported on macOS only. |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | Conditions: AgentConnected and Reachable. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.hostIP` | string |  |  |  | HostIP is the first candidate the dial-back verified. It is set only while Reachable is True. |
| `status.ingressPort` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | IngressPort is the agent's TLS ingress port. |
| `status.ingressSPKI` | string |  |  |  | IngressSPKI is the base64 SHA-256 digest of the agent ingress certificate's public key, as the agent reports it. The dial-back and the relay complete TLS only against this key; the dial-back also requires the certificate the agent reports, which must name this MetalNode's UID. |
| `status.lastHeartbeatTime` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastHeartbeatTime is the Lease's renewTime, accurate to about a minute: it advances when AgentConnected changes or when it is more than 60 seconds behind. The Lease holds the exact time. |
| `status.observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | ObservedGeneration is the spec generation this status reflects. |
| `status.verification` | object |  |  |  | Verification is the dial-back bookkeeping. |
| `status.verification.consecutiveFailures` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | ConsecutiveFailures counts checks in a row that verified no address; it sets the retry backoff. |
| `status.verification.inputHash` | string |  |  |  | InputHash identifies the pin, ingress certificate, port, candidate list and verifier placement the last completed check ran against. A change starts a new check. |
| `status.verification.lastCheckTime` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastCheckTime is when the last completed check finished. |
| `status.verification.lastStartTime` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastStartTime is when the controller last started a verifier pod. Starts are at least 30 seconds apart, whatever the Mac reports. |
| `status.verification.podUID` | string |  |  |  | PodUID is the UID of the verifier pod the controller last created. Only that pod's results are read. |

### Model

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `Model` |
| API versions | `inference.llmkube.dev/v1alpha1` |
| Scope | Namespaced |
| Plural | `models` |
| Short names | `mdl` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/inference.llmkube.dev_models.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/inference.llmkube.dev_models.yaml) |

#### Model printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Phase | `string` | `.status.phase` | 0 |  |
| Size | `string` | `.status.size` | 0 |  |
| Accelerator | `string` | `.spec.hardware.accelerator` | 0 |  |
| Arch | `string` | `.status.gguf.architecture` | 1 |  |
| Age | `date` | `.metadata.creationTimestamp` | 0 |  |

#### Model spec

spec defines the desired state of Model

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.attestation` | object |  |  |  | Attestation references a Socair attestation for this Model's artifact: a DSSE-signed in-toto statement whose subject digest must equal spec.sha256. When the operator runs with `--model-attestation=enforce`, a Model without an attestation that a trusted key signed and the policy admits never becomes Ready, so no InferenceService serves it. With `--model-attestation=warn` the result is reported on the AttestationVerified condition without blocking. |
| `spec.attestation.configMapKeyRef` | object | yes |  |  | ConfigMapKeyRef selects the key, in a ConfigMap in the Model's namespace, that holds the attestation's DSSE envelope (the attestation.dsse.json that `socair sign` writes). |
| `spec.attestation.configMapKeyRef.key` | string | yes |  |  | The key to select from the ConfigMap's Data field. Keys in the BinaryData field are not currently propagated to container env vars. |
| `spec.attestation.configMapKeyRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.attestation.configMapKeyRef.optional` | boolean |  |  |  | Specify whether the ConfigMap or its key must be defined |
| `spec.downloadConcurrency` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 1; max 16 | DownloadConcurrency is how many spec.files and spec.mmproj entries the downloader stages at once. Unset stages one at a time. A file's close, SHA256 check and publish then overlap the next file's transfer. It requires spec.files and a source the downloader stages: pvc:// and oci:// are mounted, not downloaded. On a FUSE cache that holds each open file on the node's disk (blobfuse2's file cache), every file in flight needs its size free there; downloadMaxInFlightSize caps that. |
| `spec.downloadMaxInFlightSize` | string |  |  | pattern `^([1-9][0-9]{0,14}\|[1-9][0-9]{0,5}(Ki\|Mi\|Gi\|Ti\|k\|M\|G\|T))$` | DownloadMaxInFlightSize caps the combined size of the files the downloadConcurrency workers hold at once, as a quantity such as "90Gi". A file starts only while the size in flight plus its own fits, or when nothing else is in flight, so a file larger than the cap still stages, alone. Files start in list order. A file present in the cache counts its size on disk, and one whose size the origin does not report counts the whole cap. Set it to the node disk a FUSE cache may fill. It takes up to 15 digits of bytes, or up to 6 digits with a suffix up to Ti, so every admitted value fits the downloader's 64-bit arithmetic. |
| `spec.fileSha256` | map of string |  |  | max properties 256 | FileSHA256 is a per-file SHA256 map for multi-file staging, keyed by file entry (the exact path or URL in spec.files, or spec.mmproj). Only listed files are verified; a file with no entry is staged as before. It is mutually exclusive with spec.sha256, and requires concrete file paths: a glob cannot be keyed to an unknown expanded name, and a pre-staged oci:// artifact carries its own digest, so both are rejected at admission (#1978). |
| `spec.files` | array of string |  |  | max items 256 | Files lists model weight artifacts to stage from Source. Entries are repo-relative for repository sources, or absolute http(s)/s3 object URLs. Resolve URLs on huggingface.co retain the path within their repository; S3 URLs retain the object key. Other HTTP URLs, including explicit HF_ENDPOINT mirror URLs, use the basename. Conflicting destination paths are rejected. Explicit URLs use per-origin credentials and require in-cluster staging (not the Metal agent or pre-staged volumes). The first entry is the primary model file passed to the runtime. When Files is empty, Source must name a single object directly. |
| `spec.format` | string |  | `"gguf"` | one of `"gguf"`, `"mlx"`, `"safetensors"`, `"pytorch"`, `"custom"` | Format specifies the model file format. "gguf" is used with the llama-server runtime; "mlx" is used with the oMLX runtime; "safetensors", "pytorch", and "custom" are used with the generic runtime. |
| `spec.hardware` | object |  |  |  | Hardware specifies hardware acceleration preferences |
| `spec.hardware.accelerator` | string |  | `"cpu"` | one of `"cpu"`, `"metal"`, `"cuda"`, `"rocm"`, `"intel"`, `"vulkan"` | Accelerator specifies the type of hardware acceleration. "vulkan" covers AMD and Intel GPUs using the Vulkan runtime (gpu.vendor: amd/intel + gpu.runtime: vulkan). When set to "vulkan" the readiness-check path uses devic.es/dri-render as the GPU resource name instead of amd.com/gpu or nvidia.com/gpu. "rocm" is the HIP compute tier for AMD GPUs (LLMKube's own hardware-validated ROCm llama.cpp image, see #701); like "vulkan" it resolves to devic.es/dri-render rather than amd.com/gpu, since the two runtimes share the same physical device on single-GPU AMD nodes. The tiers differ only in the container image they select. |
| `spec.hardware.gpu` | object |  |  |  | GPU specifies GPU device requirements |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.count` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0; max 8 | Count is the number of GPUs the model uses. It sizes the runtime (for example vLLM's `--tensor-parallel-size` when above 1) and, without resourceClaims, requests that many devices through the device plugin as a container limit (nvidia.com/gpu by default). With resourceClaims set the claims allocate the devices and no device-plugin limit is added; set Count to the number of devices the claims provide so the runtime is sized to match. |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.enabled` | boolean |  |  |  | Enabled indicates whether GPU acceleration is enabled |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.layers` | integer (int32) |  |  | min -1 | Layers specifies layer offloading configuration for multi-GPU Format: number of layers to offload to GPU (e.g., 32 for full offload on 7B model) -1 means auto-detect optimal layer split |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.memory` | string |  |  |  | Memory specifies minimum GPU memory required per GPU (e.g., "8Gi", "16Gi") |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.resourceClaims` | array of object |  |  | max items 16 | ResourceClaims defines DRA (Dynamic Resource Allocation) claims for GPU devices. Uses resource.k8s.io/v1 PodResourceClaim format. Each claim must have exactly one of resourceClaimName or resourceClaimTemplateName set. Mutually exclusive with resourceName. When set, the operator does not add a device-plugin GPU limit; Count still sizes the runtime. Has 3 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.resourceName` | string |  |  | pattern `^[a-z0-9.\-]+/[a-z0-9._\-]+$` | ResourceName overrides the extended resource the operator requests for this Model's pods. Defaults are derived from Vendor: nvidia -&gt; nvidia.com/gpu amd -&gt; amd.com/gpu intel -&gt; gpu.intel.com/i915 Set this for non-default device plugins (e.g. squat/generic-device-plugin advertising `squat.ai/dri-render`, NVIDIA MIG slices). When set, this value also drives the GPU toleration unless TolerationKey is provided explicitly. |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.runtime` | string |  |  | one of `"vulkan"`, `"rocm"` | Runtime selects the GPU compute backend the operator schedules for this Model, independent of the Vendor field. It exists so `vendor: amd` is not overloaded to mean both "ROCm" and "Vulkan". |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.sharding` | object |  |  |  | Sharding defines how to shard the model across multiple GPUs Only applicable when Count &gt; 1 Has 2 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.tolerationKey` | string |  |  | pattern `^[a-z0-9.\-]+/[a-z0-9._\-]+$` | TolerationKey overrides the taint key the operator tolerates when scheduling GPU pods. Defaults to ResourceName (or the vendor default resource name when ResourceName is unset), so in most cases this can be left empty. |
| `spec.hardware.gpu.vendor` | string |  | `"nvidia"` | one of `"nvidia"`, `"amd"`, `"intel"` | Vendor specifies GPU vendor preference (nvidia, amd, intel) Future-proof for multi-vendor support |
| `spec.hardware.memoryBudget` | string |  |  |  | MemoryBudget is an absolute memory limit for the model process (e.g., "24Gi", "8192Mi"). When set, it takes precedence over MemoryFraction and the agent-level `--memory-fraction` flag. Parsed via resource.ParseQuantity(). |
| `spec.hardware.memoryFraction` | number |  |  |  | MemoryFraction is the fraction of total system memory to budget for this model's inference process (0.0–1.0). Takes precedence over the agent-level `--memory-fraction` flag but not MemoryBudget. |
| `spec.mmproj` | string |  |  |  | Mmproj is an optional multimodal projector file to stage from Source and pass to runtimes that support projector arguments. |
| `spec.modelCache` | object |  |  |  | ModelCache names the cache claim this Model is staged into, with the same fields an InferenceService's spec.modelCache carries. |
| `spec.modelCache.claimName` | string |  |  | min length 1; max length 253 | ClaimName names a pre-existing PersistentVolumeClaim in the InferenceService's namespace to use as the writable model cache volume. Weights land under the usual &lt;cacheKey&gt;/ subdirectory of the claim, so RefreshPolicy and cache-key semantics are unchanged and multiple models can share one claim without colliding. The claim must already exist: when it is missing the InferenceService is marked Degraded rather than silently falling back to the shared cache. Ignored for pvc:// model sources (already staged, read-only, no download). Node alignment of RWO/local claims (via nodeSelector) is the user's responsibility. |
| `spec.modelCache.persistence` | string |  |  | one of `"Cached"`, `"Ephemeral"` | Persistence selects whether weights survive a Pod restart. |
| `spec.modelCache.prefetch` | boolean |  |  |  | Prefetch asks for the Model's weights to be staged into this cache claim by a prefetch Job before any serving pod consumes them. |
| `spec.modelCache.size` | string |  |  | min length 1; max length 64 | Size overrides the operator's `--model-cache-size` flag (100Gi when the flag is unset too) for the model-cache PVC this operator creates for the service (#2448). A cluster serving both small and large models can then size each service's cache for its own model rather than for the largest one, without hand-managing a claimName PVC per service. |
| `spec.modelCache.storageClassName` | string |  |  | min length 1; max length 63 | StorageClassName overrides the operator's `--model-cache-storage-class` flag for the model-cache PVC this operator creates for the service (#1963). A mixed cluster can then cache on local disk where a cloud-default class cannot attach, without hand-managing a claimName PVC per service. |
| `spec.prefetch` | boolean |  |  |  | Prefetch, when true on a remote (http/https/hf) source, makes the operator download the artifact into a model cache PVC ahead of any InferenceService, via an owner-ref'd Job that reuses the serving init-container download stack. The Model reads Downloading until the Job completes and Ready after it, so a referencing service starts from a cache hit. With prefetchOCI set, the Job publishes to a registry instead and consumers use a separate oci:// Model with the published digest. A glob in spec.files is expanded from the repository listing before the Job is built, as the serving path expands it. |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity` | object |  |  |  | PrefetchAffinity is applied to the prefetch Job's pod alongside PrefetchNodeSelector and PrefetchTolerations, and governs nothing else. It expresses what a selector cannot: keep the download off the GPU nodes (a NotIn or DoesNotExist node affinity), a list of allowed hosts, or a preference rather than a requirement. |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity.nodeAffinity` | object |  |  |  | Describes node affinity scheduling rules for the pod. |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity.nodeAffinity.preferredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution` | array of object |  |  |  | The scheduler will prefer to schedule pods to nodes that satisfy the affinity expressions specified by this field, but it may choose a node that violates one or more of the expressions. The node that is most preferred is the one with the greatest sum of weights, i.e. for each node that meets all of the scheduling requirements (resource request, requiredDuringScheduling affinity expressions, etc.), compute a sum by iterating through the elements of this field and adding "weight" to the sum if the node matches the corresponding matchExpressions; the node(s) with the highest sum are the most preferred. Has 10 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity.nodeAffinity.requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution` | object |  |  |  | If the affinity requirements specified by this field are not met at scheduling time, the pod will not be scheduled onto the node. If the affinity requirements specified by this field cease to be met at some point during pod execution (e.g. due to an update), the system may or may not try to eventually evict the pod from its node. Has 9 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity.podAffinity` | object |  |  |  | Describes pod affinity scheduling rules (e.g. co-locate this pod in the same node, zone, etc. as some other pod(s)). |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity.podAffinity.preferredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution` | array of object |  |  |  | The scheduler will prefer to schedule pods to nodes that satisfy the affinity expressions specified by this field, but it may choose a node that violates one or more of the expressions. The node that is most preferred is the one with the greatest sum of weights, i.e. for each node that meets all of the scheduling requirements (resource request, requiredDuringScheduling affinity expressions, etc.), compute a sum by iterating through the elements of this field and adding "weight" to the sum if the node has pods which matches the corresponding podAffinityTerm; the node(s) with the highest sum are the most preferred. Has 18 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity.podAffinity.requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution` | array of object |  |  |  | If the affinity requirements specified by this field are not met at scheduling time, the pod will not be scheduled onto the node. If the affinity requirements specified by this field cease to be met at some point during pod execution (e.g. due to a pod label update), the system may or may not try to eventually evict the pod from its node. When there are multiple elements, the lists of nodes corresponding to each podAffinityTerm are intersected, i.e. all terms must be satisfied. Has 16 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity.podAntiAffinity` | object |  |  |  | Describes pod anti-affinity scheduling rules (e.g. avoid putting this pod in the same node, zone, etc. as some other pod(s)). |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity.podAntiAffinity.preferredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution` | array of object |  |  |  | The scheduler will prefer to schedule pods to nodes that satisfy the anti-affinity expressions specified by this field, but it may choose a node that violates one or more of the expressions. The node that is most preferred is the one with the greatest sum of weights, i.e. for each node that meets all of the scheduling requirements (resource request, requiredDuringScheduling anti-affinity expressions, etc.), compute a sum by iterating through the elements of this field and subtracting "weight" from the sum if the node has pods which matches the corresponding podAffinityTerm; the node(s) with the highest sum are the most preferred. Has 18 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.prefetchAffinity.podAntiAffinity.requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution` | array of object |  |  |  | If the anti-affinity requirements specified by this field are not met at scheduling time, the pod will not be scheduled onto the node. If the anti-affinity requirements specified by this field cease to be met at some point during pod execution (e.g. due to a pod label update), the system may or may not try to eventually evict the pod from its node. When there are multiple elements, the lists of nodes corresponding to each podAffinityTerm are intersected, i.e. all terms must be satisfied. Has 16 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.prefetchNodeSelector` | map of string |  |  |  | PrefetchNodeSelector is applied to the prefetch Job's pod and governs nothing else: like PrefetchTolerations it says where the download may run, not where the model will be served. It exists because prefetch honors a claimName override (#1676), and a node-pinned claim (a WaitForFirstConsumer local-path PVC) binds to whichever node the prefetch pod schedules to; without a selector the PV can bind to a node the InferenceService never runs on (#1964). |
| `spec.prefetchOCI` | object |  |  |  | PrefetchOCI publishes downloaded files as a container image instead of staging the Model into a PVC. Consumers use a separate oci:// Model pinned to the published digest. Setting this block enables publication without prefetch: true. At most 64 resolved files (including mmproj) may be published. Service-requested PVC prefetch remains independent. |
| `spec.prefetchOCI.ephemeralStorage` | string | yes |  |  | EphemeralStorage is the required node-local disk budget for downloading all selected files, with headroom for partial downloads and container logs. Applied to requests and limits on each Job container and to emptyDir.sizeLimit. No default is safe for both small and very large models. |
| `spec.prefetchOCI.image` | string | yes |  | min length 1 | Image is a fully qualified registry/repository:tag destination (no oci:// prefix). |
| `spec.prefetchOCI.pushSecretRef` | object |  |  |  | PushSecretRef names a kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson Secret in the Model's namespace. It is mounted only in the publisher, never in the downloader. |
| `spec.prefetchOCI.pushSecretRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.prefetchTolerations` | array of object |  |  |  | PrefetchTolerations are applied to the prefetch Job's pod, and govern nothing else: they say where the download may run, not where the model will be served. A fleet whose GPU nodes are tainted needs the prefetch download to schedule onto those nodes when the shared cache lives on node-local storage (#1621). Inheriting from an InferenceService would be ill-defined here: prefetch runs before the first InferenceService exists by design. |
| `spec.prefetchTolerations[].effect` | string |  |  |  | Effect indicates the taint effect to match. Empty means match all taint effects. When specified, allowed values are NoSchedule, PreferNoSchedule and NoExecute. |
| `spec.prefetchTolerations[].key` | string |  |  |  | Key is the taint key that the toleration applies to. Empty means match all taint keys. If the key is empty, operator must be Exists; this combination means to match all values and all keys. |
| `spec.prefetchTolerations[].operator` | string |  |  |  | Operator represents a key's relationship to the value. Valid operators are Exists, Equal, Lt, and Gt. Defaults to Equal. Exists is equivalent to wildcard for value, so that a pod can tolerate all taints of a particular category. Lt and Gt perform numeric comparisons (requires feature gate TaintTolerationComparisonOperators). |
| `spec.prefetchTolerations[].tolerationSeconds` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | TolerationSeconds represents the period of time the toleration (which must be of effect NoExecute, otherwise this field is ignored) tolerates the taint. By default, it is not set, which means tolerate the taint forever (do not evict). Zero and negative values will be treated as 0 (evict immediately) by the system. |
| `spec.prefetchTolerations[].value` | string |  |  |  | Value is the taint value the toleration matches to. If the operator is Exists, the value should be empty, otherwise just a regular string. |
| `spec.quantization` | string |  |  |  | Quantization describes the quantization level (e.g., Q4_0, Q5_K_M, F16) |
| `spec.refreshPolicy` | string |  | `"IfNotPresent"` | one of `"IfNotPresent"`, `"OnChange"` | RefreshPolicy controls whether a cached model file is re-fetched when the upstream source changes. |
| `spec.resources` | object |  |  |  | Resources defines resource requirements for running the model |
| `spec.resources.cpu` | string |  |  |  | CPU specifies CPU requirements (e.g., "2" or "2000m") |
| `spec.resources.memory` | string |  |  |  | Memory specifies memory requirements (e.g., "4Gi") |
| `spec.sha256` | string |  |  | pattern `^[a-fA-F0-9]{64}$` | SHA256 is the expected SHA256 hash of the model file for integrity verification. When set, a single-file artifact verifies its bytes against this hash: a downloaded artifact before it becomes the cache, and a pre-staged pvc:// artifact at pod start. Setting it together with Files or Mmproj, or on a pre-staged oci:// source, is rejected at admission; use spec.fileSha256 to pin the files of a multi-file staging set (#1965, #1978, #1979). |
| `spec.source` | string |  |  | pattern `^(https?\|file\|pvc\|hf\|s3\|oci\|hailo)://.*\|^/[^\s]+$\|^[a-zA-Z0-9][\w\-\.\/]+$` | Source defines where to obtain the model. It may be omitted when every files entry is an absolute http(s) or s3 object URL. For GGUF models: URL or path to a .gguf file. For MLX models: local directory path containing the model (config.json, weights). Supported schemes: `http://`, `https://`, file://, pvc://, hf://, s3://, oci://, or absolute paths. Examples: - `https://huggingface.co/org/repo/resolve/main/model.gguf` - file:///mnt/models/model.gguf - /mnt/models/model.gguf (air-gapped deployments) - pvc://my-models-pvc/path/to/model.gguf (pre-staged on a PersistentVolumeClaim) - s3://my-bucket/models/llama-3.1-8b-q4_k_m.gguf (S3-compatible object store) - oci://registry.example.com/models/llama-3.1-8b@sha256:... (mounted by a Kubernetes ImageVolume; requires Kubernetes &gt;= 1.36 and containerd &gt;= 2.1.0 or CRI-O &gt;= 1.31 on the serving node; pin by digest) - /mnt/models/Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct-4bit (MLX model directory) - hailo://qwen2.5-instruct:1.5b (a HEF in Hailo's GenAI model zoo, pulled and served by hailo-ollama through the host agent's ollama runtime; experimental, needs hardware.accelerator: metal) |
| `spec.sourceSecretRef` | object |  |  |  | SourceSecretRef names a Secret (in the Model's namespace) whose keys are wired as env into the model-downloader init container. |
| `spec.sourceSecretRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |

#### Model status

status defines the observed state of Model

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.acceleratorReady` | boolean |  |  |  | AcceleratorReady indicates if hardware acceleration is configured and ready |
| `status.cacheKey` | string |  |  |  | CacheKey is the SHA256 hash prefix of the source URL used for cache storage Models with the same source URL share the same cache entry |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | conditions represent the current state of the Model resource. Each condition has a unique type and reflects the status of a specific aspect of the resource. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.gguf` | object |  |  |  | GGUF contains metadata extracted from the GGUF file header |
| `status.gguf.architecture` | string |  |  |  | Architecture is the model architecture (e.g., "llama", "mistral", "phi") |
| `status.gguf.contextLength` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | ContextLength is the maximum context length (tokens) |
| `status.gguf.embeddingSize` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | EmbeddingSize is the embedding dimension size |
| `status.gguf.fileVersion` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | FileVersion is the GGUF file format version |
| `status.gguf.headCount` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | HeadCount is the number of attention heads |
| `status.gguf.headCountKV` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | HeadCountKV is the number of key/value attention heads (grouped-query attention). 0 means the GGUF did not provide a usable scalar value (absent, array-valued, or written by an older controller); consumers must treat 0 as unknown and fall back to the embedding-width estimate. |
| `status.gguf.keyLength` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | KeyLength is the per-head key dimension from the GGUF attention metadata. 0 means the GGUF did not provide a usable scalar value; consumers treat it as unknown and derive it when needed. |
| `status.gguf.layerCount` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | LayerCount is the number of transformer layers/blocks |
| `status.gguf.license` | string |  |  |  | License is the license identifier extracted from the GGUF file metadata |
| `status.gguf.modelName` | string |  |  |  | ModelName is the model name as stored in the GGUF file |
| `status.gguf.quantization` | string |  |  |  | Quantization is the quantization type (e.g., "Q4_K_M", "Q5_K_M") |
| `status.gguf.tensorCount` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | TensorCount is the number of tensors in the model |
| `status.gguf.valueLength` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | ValueLength is the per-head value dimension from the GGUF attention metadata. 0 means the GGUF did not provide a usable scalar value; consumers treat it as unknown and derive it when needed. |
| `status.lastRevalidated` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastRevalidated is the timestamp of the last upstream revalidation check. Revalidation is cadence-gated so the controller does not issue a HEAD on every reconcile. |
| `status.lastUpdated` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastUpdated is the timestamp of the last status update |
| `status.path` | string |  |  |  | Path represents the local path where the model is stored |
| `status.phase` | string |  |  | one of `"Pending"`, `"Downloading"`, `"Copying"`, `"Ready"`, `"Failed"` | Phase represents the current lifecycle phase of the model. Possible values: Pending, Downloading, Copying, Ready, Failed. |
| `status.prefetch` | array of object |  |  |  | Prefetch reports every cache claim this Model has been, or is being, prefetched into: one entry per claim, whether the Model asked for it (spec.prefetch) or a referencing InferenceService did (modelCache.prefetch). A service waits on the entry for its own claim. |
| `status.prefetch[].cacheKey` | string |  |  |  | CacheKey is the cache directory under the claim the files land in. |
| `status.prefetch[].claimName` | string | yes |  | min length 1 | ClaimName is the PersistentVolumeClaim the Job stages into. |
| `status.prefetch[].files` | array of string |  |  |  | Files are the cache-relative names the Job staged under cacheKey, in staging order with the primary file first. A pod that consumes the prefetched claim verifies and serves exactly these, so a later change in how a name would be derived (GGUF metadata parsed after the Job ran) cannot make it look for a file the Job wrote under another name. |
| `status.prefetch[].fingerprint` | string |  |  |  | Fingerprint identifies the source and file set the entry staged. A spec change that alters it starts a new prefetch into the same claim. |
| `status.prefetch[].jobName` | string |  |  |  | JobName is the prefetch Job driving this entry. The Job is removed a day after it finishes; the entry outlives it. |
| `status.prefetch[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastTransitionTime is when Phase last changed. |
| `status.prefetch[].message` | string |  |  |  | Message explains a Pending or Failed phase. |
| `status.prefetch[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | ObservedGeneration is the Model generation the entry was last confirmed against. A consumer treats an entry whose generation trails the Model's as not yet current. |
| `status.prefetch[].own` | boolean |  |  |  | Own marks the Model's own prefetch target (spec.prefetch), the entry that gates the Model's Ready. Entries an InferenceService requested leave it unset. |
| `status.prefetch[].phase` | string | yes |  | one of `"Pending"`, `"Running"`, `"Succeeded"`, `"Failed"` | Phase is Pending, Running, Succeeded or Failed. |
| `status.prefetchOCI` | object |  |  |  | PrefetchOCI records the published image and the files it contains. |
| `status.prefetchOCI.files` | array of string |  |  |  | Files contains image-root-relative paths, with the primary file first. |
| `status.prefetchOCI.fingerprint` | string |  |  |  | Fingerprint identifies the source, file set and destination of this publication. |
| `status.prefetchOCI.image` | string |  |  |  | Image is the published oci://registry/repository@sha256:... reference. |
| `status.prefetchOCI.jobName` | string |  |  |  |  |
| `status.prefetchOCI.message` | string |  |  |  |  |
| `status.prefetchOCI.observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  |  |  |
| `status.prefetchOCI.phase` | string | yes |  | one of `"Pending"`, `"Running"`, `"Succeeded"`, `"Failed"` | Phase is Pending, Running, Succeeded or Failed. |
| `status.sha256` | string |  |  |  | SHA256 is the computed SHA256 hash of the model file. Populated after download/copy for integrity tracking. |
| `status.size` | string |  |  |  | Size is a human-readable byte count such as "190.4 GiB": the downloaded file, the cache entry, the probed remote GGUF, or the total of the staged files when spec.files expands against a repository listing. Empty when the controller could not measure the source. |
| `status.sourceContentLength` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | SourceContentLength is the upstream size recorded at the last revalidation. For http/https sources it is the Content-Length reported by a HEAD request; for local sources it is the file size on disk. Used together with SourceETag (or mtime for local sources) to detect upstream changes. |
| `status.sourceETag` | string |  |  |  | SourceETag is the HTTP ETag recorded for the upstream source at the last revalidation. Used to detect upstream changes for http/https sources (HuggingFace serves the blob SHA as the ETag, so a moved branch is caught). |
| `status.stagedFiles` | array of string |  |  |  | StagedFiles lists the repo-relative paths staged in the model cache. Populated when spec.files or spec.mmproj are set. |

#### ModelPool spec

spec defines the desired state of ModelPool

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.default` | string |  |  |  | Default names the member to warm on a cold pool (before any request has picked an owner). When empty, the first request to arrive selects the owner. Must match one of the members' InferenceServiceRef names. |
| `spec.gpu` | integer (int32) |  | `1` | min 0 | GPU is the number of GPUs the shared slot occupies. Informational for the pool invariant (members declare their own resource requests); recorded so operators can see the slot size at a glance. |
| `spec.members` | array of object | yes |  | min items 1 | Members lists the InferenceServices that share the pool's GPU slot. Each member is an ordinary single-model InferenceService with its own image, context size, and runtime. The pool owns each member's spec.replicas: leave it out of GitOps manifests, or every sync that restores it is read as a request for that member (docs/operations/gpu-sharing.md, "GitOps"). |
| `spec.members[].inferenceServiceRef` | object | yes |  |  | InferenceServiceRef references the member InferenceService by name. The InferenceService must live in the same namespace as the ModelPool. |
| `spec.members[].inferenceServiceRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.nodeSelector` | map of string |  |  |  | NodeSelector pins the shared slot to a node (or node class). All members are expected to schedule onto the same node so they contend for the same device memory. Mirrors a standard Kubernetes node selector. |
| `spec.reclaimAfter` | string |  | `"300s"` |  | ReclaimAfter is how long the resident non-default member must be continuously idle before the slot is reclaimed for spec.default. Only consulted when SwapPolicy is "reclaim" and Default is set. It MUST be set comfortably longer than the on-demand member's typical inter-request gap: a value shorter than that gap trades cross-model request coalescing for load-side thrash, reclaiming the default only to swap it straight back out on the next on-demand request. Defaults to 300s. |
| `spec.swapBudget` | string |  | `"300s"` |  | SwapBudget bounds how long the router holds a cross-model request open while it drains the incumbent and cold-loads the target member. It is deliberately decoupled from the router's response-header (generation) timeout: a large model can take minutes to load without forcing operators to loosen the per-request generation cap for every request. When the budget elapses before the target becomes resident, the held request is failed with 503 + Retry-After (the caller can retry; the now-warm member serves the next request). Defaults to 300s. |
| `spec.swapPolicy` | string |  | `"sticky"` | one of `"sticky"`, `"reclaim"` | SwapPolicy selects how the slot owner is chosen when demand crosses model boundaries. "sticky" (default) keeps the incumbent until a different member is requested. "reclaim" additionally returns the slot to spec.default after the resident non-default member has been idle for spec.reclaimAfter. |

#### ModelPool status

status defines the observed state of ModelPool

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | Conditions represent the current state of the ModelPool resource. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.externalScale` | object |  |  |  | ExternalScale records the last scale-up of a non-resident member by a field manager outside LLMKube. It identifies the write already reported, so the same scale-up seen on a later pass is not reported again, and times the ExternallyScaled condition's quiet period. |
| `status.externalScale.manager` | string | yes |  |  | Manager is the field manager that owns the member's spec.replicas, as recorded in its managedFields (for example kustomize-controller or kubectl). |
| `status.externalScale.member` | string | yes |  |  | Member is the member InferenceService that was scaled up. |
| `status.externalScale.memberGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | MemberGeneration is the member's metadata.generation when the scale-up was observed. With Member and Manager it identifies one write. |
| `status.externalScale.observedAt` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | ObservedAt is when the pool first observed this scale-up. |
| `status.members` | array of object |  |  |  | Members reports the observed state of each pool member. |
| `status.members[].name` | string | yes |  |  | Name is the member InferenceService name. |
| `status.members[].phase` | string |  |  |  | Phase mirrors the member InferenceService status.phase. |
| `status.members[].resident` | boolean |  |  |  | Resident is true when this member currently owns the shared GPU slot. |
| `status.observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | ObservedGeneration is the spec generation the status reflects. |
| `status.pendingMember` | string |  |  |  | PendingMember is the member being loaded during a swap, or empty when the pool is not mid-swap. |
| `status.phase` | string |  |  | one of `"Pending"`, `"Ready"`, `"Swapping"`, `"Degraded"` | Phase is the pool lifecycle phase: Pending, Ready, Swapping, or Degraded. |
| `status.residentIdleSince` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | ResidentIdleSince is when the resident non-default member was first observed idle under the "reclaim" swap policy. It is cleared when the member serves a request again or when spec.default becomes resident, and is used to time the reclaim of the slot back to spec.default. |
| `status.residentMember` | string |  |  |  | ResidentMember is the member that currently owns the shared GPU slot, or empty when the pool is cold (no member resident). |

#### ModelRouter printer columns

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |

#### ModelRouter spec

spec defines the desired state of ModelRouter

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.agentGatewayRef` | object |  |  |  | AgentGatewayRef identifies the pre-installed agentgateway data plane the generated InferencePool/HTTPRoute/model-extraction AgentgatewayPolicy attach to when DataPlane is "AgentGateway". Required in AgentGateway mode; ignored otherwise. The Gateway, the Gateway API Inference Extension CRDs (InferencePool at inference.networking.k8s.io/v1), the agentgateway.dev CRDs, and an Endpoint Picker (EPP) service are documented prerequisites; LLMKube does not install or own them. The agentgateway controller must run with Inference-Pool backendRef support (inferenceExtension.enabled=true in the agentgateway chart). The EndpointPicker is referenced by every generated InferencePool so agentgateway can select a model-server endpoint per request. The Gateway may be in another namespace: the generated AgentgatewayPolicy then lives in the Gateway's namespace (its targetRefs are same-namespace), is not owner-referenced, and is cleaned up via a finalizer on this ModelRouter. Because that policy targets the whole Gateway, its body-model extraction runs on EVERY request through that Gateway, including routes LLMKube did not generate. The Gateway must not already carry another PreRouting transformation policy: the router is refused (ConflictingPreRoutingPolicy) rather than silently shadowed. agentgateway keeps the OLDEST same-specificity policy's transformation, so LLMKube does not retract its policy on conflict; the condition clears when the foreign policy is removed, and the reconciler rechecks every two minutes (a foreign policy created after the router went Ready is otherwise silently shadowed). agentgateway inspects at most 2 MiB of the request body by default (its DEFAULT_BUFFER_LIMIT); larger bodies (long contexts, images) are not routed on this path - the CEL extraction fails and the model header is removed. A Gateway owner can raise the limit with a frontend.http.maxBufferSize policy, a different field that does not conflict with the extraction policy. |
| `spec.agentGatewayRef.endpointPicker` | string |  |  |  | EndpointPicker names the router-wide default EPP Service. A backend's own endpointPicker overrides it. A Gateway API Inference Extension EPP binds to exactly one InferencePool, so the default serves a router whose backends all reference one InferenceService; with several, set spec.backends\[\].endpointPicker. The Service lives in this ModelRouter's namespace: GIE v1's endpointPickerRef has no namespace field. |
| `spec.agentGatewayRef.endpointPickerPort` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 1; max 65535 | EndpointPickerPort is the port the default EPP Service listens on. Set together with endpointPicker. |
| `spec.agentGatewayRef.hostnames` | array of string |  |  | min items 1; max items 16 | Hostnames limit the generated HTTPRoute to these hosts. routing: Model only, where it or sectionName is required. |
| `spec.agentGatewayRef.name` | string | yes |  |  | Name is the agentgateway Gateway's name. |
| `spec.agentGatewayRef.namespace` | string |  |  |  | Namespace is the Gateway's namespace. Empty means the ModelRouter's own namespace. A different namespace is supported: the generated model-extraction AgentgatewayPolicy then lives in the Gateway's namespace (it cannot be owner-referenced across namespaces and is cleaned up via a finalizer on this ModelRouter), while the pools and route stay here. The pools, the referenced InferenceServices, and the EndpointPicker Service all live in this ModelRouter's namespace regardless. |
| `spec.agentGatewayRef.routing` | string |  | `"InferencePool"` | one of `"InferencePool"`, `"Model"` | Routing selects the compile target. "InferencePool" (the default) is the Gateway API Inference Extension path described on spec.agentGatewayRef. "Model" uses agentgateway's model router (v1.6.0+): one AgentgatewayModel per backend, matched on its displayName (else name), behind one catch-all HTTPRoute. It needs no Inference Extension CRDs or Endpoint Picker. spec.rules, spec.defaultRoute, external backends, resolution: endpoint, and per-backend endpoint pickers are refused in this mode. |
| `spec.agentGatewayRef.sectionName` | string |  |  | pattern `^[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*$`; min length 1; max length 253 | SectionName names the Gateway listener the generated HTTPRoute attaches to. routing: Model only, where it or hostnames is required, because the route takes every path on its listeners. |
| `spec.backends` | array of object | yes |  | min items 1 | Backends are the candidate destinations the router can dispatch to. Order is not significant; selection is rule-driven. At least one backend must be declared. |
| `spec.backends[].balancing` | object |  |  |  | Balancing places requests across this backend's pods by load and keeps a session on its pod until it runs hot. Requires resolution: endpoint. Unset keeps round-robin. Applies to the Proxy data plane only; ignored by the Gateway and AgentGateway data planes. |
| `spec.backends[].balancing.mode` | string |  | `"RoundRobin"` | one of `"RoundRobin"`, `"LoadAware"` | Mode is RoundRobin (today's behavior) or LoadAware: the pod with the least work, read from the engine's own metrics where LLMKube has a profile for the runtime (vLLM) and from in-flight requests otherwise. |
| `spec.backends[].balancing.prefixAffinity` | object |  |  |  | PrefixAffinity sends a request to the pod most likely to hold its prompt's prefix in KV cache, traded against that pod's load. The router remembers which prompt blocks it sent to each pod, so it needs no session header and no engine support. Set it (an empty object takes the defaults) to turn it on. It requires mode: LoadAware; with RoundRobin it is ignored and the router emits a PrefixAffinityIgnored warning event. Has 2 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.backends[].balancing.sessionAffinity` | object |  |  |  | SessionAffinity places a new session on the least-loaded pod. The session stays there unless that pod is queuing and clearly busier than another pod, and then it stays where it moved. It requires mode: LoadAware; with RoundRobin it is ignored and the router emits a SessionAffinityIgnored warning event. Has 3 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.backends[].capabilities` | array of string |  |  |  | Capabilities advertised by this backend. Rules can require capabilities (e.g. \["tools", "vision", "long-context"\]) to filter candidates. |
| `spec.backends[].costPerMillionTokens` | object |  |  |  | CostPerMillionTokens is informational. Used for cost-aware routing metrics and audit-log enrichment. Values are USD. |
| `spec.backends[].costPerMillionTokens.completionUSD` | string |  |  | pattern `^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$` | CompletionUSD is the cost per million completion (output) tokens, in USD. |
| `spec.backends[].costPerMillionTokens.promptUSD` | string |  |  | pattern `^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$` | PromptUSD is the cost per million prompt (input) tokens, in USD. |
| `spec.backends[].displayName` | string |  |  |  | DisplayName is an optional freeform label published as the model id on /v1/models and used by BackendNameMatch to resolve a request's model field to a backend. When unset, Name is used for both purposes (current behavior). This lets the k8s-safe Name differ from the user-facing model identifier (e.g. Name "claude-opus-4" with DisplayName "claude-opus-4-20250514"). |
| `spec.backends[].endpointPicker` | object |  |  |  | EndpointPicker selects the Endpoint Picker (EPP) that chooses a pod for this backend's InferencePool on dataPlane: AgentGateway. When unset, the backend uses the picker another backend on the same InferenceService names, else the router-wide spec.agentGatewayRef.endpointPicker. A Gateway API Inference Extension EPP binds to exactly one InferencePool, so a router with backends on several InferenceServices sets a picker per backend. Ignored by the Proxy and Gateway data planes. |
| `spec.backends[].endpointPicker.service` | object |  |  |  | Service names an EPP Service in the ModelRouter's namespace. GIE v1's endpointPickerRef has no namespace field, so the pool references it same-namespace. Has 2 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.backends[].external` | object |  |  |  | External describes an out-of-cluster provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, or a LiteLLM proxy). Mutually exclusive with InferenceServiceRef. |
| `spec.backends[].external.credentialsSecretRef` | object |  |  |  | CredentialsSecretRef points to a Kubernetes Secret containing the provider credentials. Conventional keys: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, LITELLM_MASTER_KEY. The router-proxy reads these as environment variables. Has 1 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.backends[].external.model` | string | yes |  |  | Model is the upstream model identifier passed through to the provider (e.g. "claude-opus-4-7", "gpt-5", a LiteLLM model alias). |
| `spec.backends[].external.provider` | string | yes |  | one of `"anthropic"`, `"openai"`, `"bedrock"`, `"vertex_ai"`, `"litellm"` | Provider identifies the upstream API surface. For "litellm", URL must point at a running LiteLLM proxy speaking OpenAI-compatible API. For first-party providers, URL is optional (provider defaults apply). |
| `spec.backends[].external.url` | string |  |  |  | URL is the base URL for the provider. Required for "litellm"; optional for first-party providers, which use their published default. |
| `spec.backends[].healthCheck` | object |  |  |  | HealthCheck overrides the default health probe applied to this backend by the router-proxy. |
| `spec.backends[].healthCheck.intervalSeconds` | integer (int32) |  | `10` | min 1 | IntervalSeconds is how often the router-proxy probes the backend. |
| `spec.backends[].healthCheck.path` | string |  |  |  | Path is the HTTP path probed for health. Defaults to "/health" for local backends and to the provider's documented health route for external providers. |
| `spec.backends[].healthCheck.timeoutSeconds` | integer (int32) |  | `2` | min 1 | TimeoutSeconds is the maximum time a single probe may take. |
| `spec.backends[].inferenceServiceRef` | object |  |  |  | InferenceServiceRef references an in-cluster InferenceService. Mutually exclusive with External. |
| `spec.backends[].inferenceServiceRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.backends[].modelInfo` | object |  |  |  | ModelInfo describes what this backend's model accepts and how much context it holds. The proxy publishes it on the backend's /v1/models entry, where clients decide whether to send images and how to size their context. Informational only: the proxy does not enforce it. Unset, the entry carries only the model id. Applies to the Proxy data plane only; the Gateway and AgentGateway data planes ignore it. |
| `spec.backends[].modelInfo.contextLength` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 1 | ContextLength is the context window one request gets, in tokens. When unset, the controller fills it from the InferenceService: vllmConfig.maxModelLen (or `--max-model-len` in extraArgs) for vLLM, and contextSize divided across parallelSlots for llama.cpp. Other runtimes, and llama.cpp extraArgs that set those flags, publish none. |
| `spec.backends[].modelInfo.inputModalities` | array of string |  |  | max items 5 | InputModalities the model accepts. When unset, a backend whose capabilities include "vision" publishes \["text", "image"\], and any other backend \["text"\]. When set, it wins over capabilities. |
| `spec.backends[].modelInfo.outputModalities` | array of string |  |  | max items 5 | OutputModalities the model produces. Defaults to \["text"\]. |
| `spec.backends[].name` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,62}$` | Name is the stable identifier used by rules and observability labels. Must be lowercase alphanumeric or '-'. |
| `spec.backends[].resolution` | string |  | `"service"` | one of `"service"`, `"endpoint"` | Resolution selects how a local (inferenceServiceRef) backend's upstream address is resolved. "service" (the default) dispatches to the InferenceService's ClusterIP Service DNS name, so a keep-alive client can pin to one pod through conntrack. "endpoint" has the router-proxy track the ready pods behind the Service itself and balance requests across them. External backends ignore this field. |
| `spec.backends[].tier` | string |  | `"local"` | one of `"local"`, `"cloud"` | Tier classifies the backend for rule matching. "local" backends are served from inside the cluster; "cloud" backends egress the cluster boundary. Fail-closed rules can only route to local-tier backends. |
| `spec.backends[].timeout` | string |  |  |  | Timeout caps how long the proxy waits for this backend to begin sending response headers. When set it overrides the proxy default for dispatches that target this backend. Resolution order at dispatch time: rule.timeout \|\| backend.timeout \|\| proxy default (ModelRouter.spec.proxy.responseHeaderTimeout). Useful when backends in the same router have wildly different P99 envelopes (in-cluster vLLM vs Anthropic global LB). |
| `spec.backends[].weight` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | Weight is used for the "weighted" routing strategy. Higher values receive proportionally more traffic. Ignored for other strategies. Default 1 when unset. 0 drains the backend from weighted rules: it receives no traffic from them, even while every other backend in the rule is unavailable (the Gateway API meaning of a zero weight, which both data planes follow). A weighted rule needs at least one backend with a positive weight. |
| `spec.dataPlane` | string |  | `"Proxy"` | one of `"Proxy"`, `"Gateway"`, `"AgentGateway"` | DataPlane selects how this ModelRouter serves traffic. |
| `spec.defaultRoute` | string |  |  |  | DefaultRoute names a backend used when no rule matches. Must reference the Name of an entry in Backends. |
| `spec.defaultRouteStrategy` | string |  | `"Static"` | one of `"Static"`, `"BackendNameMatch"` | DefaultRouteStrategy decides what happens when no rule matches. "Static" (default) routes to DefaultRoute. "BackendNameMatch" first tries to match the request's model to a backend Name, falling back to DefaultRoute only if none matches. BackendNameMatch makes every backend, including cloud-tier ones, directly addressable by name; sensitive-data protection still relies on a matching failClosed rule, which is evaluated first and therefore continues to gate before any name match. |
| `spec.endpoint` | object |  |  |  | Endpoint defines the Kubernetes Service the router-proxy is exposed through. Mirrors the shape used by InferenceService. |
| `spec.endpoint.apiKeySecretRef` | object |  |  |  | APIKeySecretRef names a Secret key holding the API key callers must present as "Authorization: Bearer &lt;key&gt;". vLLM reads it as VLLM_API_KEY; SGLang's launcher writes LLMKUBE_API_KEY to a private, memory-backed config file and unsets the variable before starting SGLang. kubectl exec sessions still receive LLMKUBE_API_KEY from the container's Secret reference. The key stays out of the pod spec and OS process arguments, but SGLang may still print api_key in its startup log (verified on v0.5.19). Treat pod logs and any downstream log storage as credentials until upstream redacts api_key and admin_api_key. vLLM does not have this command-line/log exposure. Both runtimes leave /health and /metrics open, so probes keep working. Supported for runtime vllm and sglang. A key change takes effect when the pods restart. An `--api-key` already in extraArgs wins; LLMKube then adds nothing. Cannot be combined with gateway.enabled: the gateway does not forward the key. Not supported on ModelRouter. |
| `spec.endpoint.apiKeySecretRef.key` | string | yes |  |  | The key of the secret to select from. Must be a valid secret key. |
| `spec.endpoint.apiKeySecretRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.endpoint.apiKeySecretRef.optional` | boolean |  |  |  | Specify whether the Secret or its key must be defined |
| `spec.endpoint.externalTrafficPolicy` | string |  |  | one of `"Cluster"`, `"Local"` | ExternalTrafficPolicy sets the Service's externalTrafficPolicy. Local preserves the client source IP on a NodePort or LoadBalancer, which an IP allowlist (NetworkPolicy) needs. Unset keeps the Kubernetes default (Cluster). Requires endpoint.type NodePort or LoadBalancer. |
| `spec.endpoint.gateway` | object |  |  |  | Gateway opts this InferenceService into Envoy AI Gateway exposure. When set and Enabled, the operator generates the Backend / AIServiceBackend / AIGatewayRoute resources that front this service through a pre-installed Envoy AI Gateway. nil (the default) preserves today's behavior (no gateway resources). The Envoy AI Gateway stack and the referenced Gateway are a documented prerequisite; LLMKube does not install or own them. |
| `spec.endpoint.gateway.enabled` | boolean |  |  |  | Enabled is the opt-in switch. When false (or when Gateway is nil), the operator generates no gateway resources for this InferenceService. |
| `spec.endpoint.gateway.gatewayRef` | object | yes |  |  | GatewayRef identifies the pre-installed Gateway (gateway.networking.k8s.io) the generated AIGatewayRoute attaches to. The Gateway typically lives in a dedicated gateway namespace; cross-namespace attachment requires the Gateway listener's allowedRoutes.namespaces to permit this InferenceService's namespace (a documented prerequisite for the MVP; the operator does not generate ReferenceGrants or touch the listener). Has 2 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.endpoint.gateway.modelName` | string |  |  |  | ModelName is the OpenAI "model" string clients send, matched by the generated route rule (the x-ai-eg-model header the gateway's ext_proc populates from the request body). Defaults to ModelRef, falling back to the InferenceService name when ModelRef is empty. |
| `spec.endpoint.nodePort` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 30000; max 32767 | NodePort is the specific NodePort to pin when endpoint.type is NodePort. If set, the Service will use this exact port instead of auto-assigning from the 30000-32767 range. This provides a stable external endpoint across redeployments. |
| `spec.endpoint.path` | string |  | `"/v1/chat/completions"` |  | Path is the HTTP path for the inference endpoint |
| `spec.endpoint.port` | integer (int32) |  | `8080` | min 1; max 65535 | Port is the service port |
| `spec.endpoint.type` | string |  | `"ClusterIP"` | one of `"ClusterIP"`, `"NodePort"`, `"LoadBalancer"` | Type is the Kubernetes service type (ClusterIP, NodePort, LoadBalancer) |
| `spec.gatewayRef` | object |  |  |  | GatewayRef identifies the pre-installed Gateway (gateway.networking.k8s.io) the generated AIGatewayRoute attaches to when DataPlane is "Gateway". Required in Gateway mode; ignored in Proxy mode. The Gateway and the Envoy AI Gateway stack are a documented prerequisite; LLMKube does not install or own them. Cross-namespace attachment requires the Gateway listener's allowedRoutes.namespaces to permit this ModelRouter's namespace. |
| `spec.gatewayRef.name` | string | yes |  |  | Name is the Gateway's name. |
| `spec.gatewayRef.namespace` | string |  |  |  | Namespace is the Gateway's namespace. Empty means the InferenceService's own namespace. |
| `spec.mcpServer` | object |  |  |  | MCPServer optionally exposes this router as a Model Context Protocol endpoint. Inactive until the Phase 3 MCP feature lands; the field is reserved in the schema for forward compatibility. |
| `spec.mcpServer.enabled` | boolean |  |  |  | Enabled toggles MCP exposure. Default false. When true (after Phase 3 lands), the router-proxy serves an MCP endpoint at /mcp using Streamable HTTP transport and OAuth 2.1. |
| `spec.policy` | object |  |  |  | Policy holds cross-cutting controls (budgets, classification, audit). |
| `spec.policy.auditLog` | object |  |  |  | AuditLog controls structured audit emission. Auditing is always on; this field tunes the destination and verbosity. |
| `spec.policy.auditLog.callerHeader` | string |  |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z0-9!#$%&'*+.^_\|~-]+$`; max length 64 | CallerHeader names a request header whose value is added to every audit entry as caller, for example X-LLMKube-Client set by each client. The client chooses the value, so it is a label rather than an identity. It is cut to 128 bytes, and characters outside printable ASCII are replaced with '?'. A header that carries a credential (Authorization, Proxy-Authorization, Cookie, X-Api-Key, Api-Key, X-Goog-Api-Key) is refused. |
| `spec.policy.auditLog.filePath` | string |  |  |  | FilePath is the destination when Sink=file. Must be writable inside the router-proxy container. Defaults to "/var/log/mlx-router/audit.log". |
| `spec.policy.auditLog.includeRequestBody` | boolean |  |  |  | IncludeRequestBody, when true, includes the OpenAI request body in every audit entry. Disabled by default for size and privacy. |
| `spec.policy.auditLog.logClientAddress` | boolean |  |  |  | LogClientAddress, when true, adds the address of the request's sender to every audit entry as client. It is the TCP peer: in-cluster callers keep their pod IP through the ClusterIP. When the peer is in TrustedProxyCIDRs, it is the first address in X-Forwarded-For, read from the right, that is not a trusted proxy. Off by default, because a client address is personal data in some deployments. |
| `spec.policy.auditLog.sink` | string |  | `"stdout"` | one of `"stdout"`, `"file"`, `"otlp"` | Sink selects the audit-log destination. "stdout" (default) emits one JSON object per line to the proxy container stdout, where it can be collected by the cluster log stack. "file" writes to FilePath inside the proxy container. "otlp" forwards entries to an OTel collector as log records. |
| `spec.policy.auditLog.trustedProxyCIDRs` | array of string |  |  | max items 32 | TrustedProxyCIDRs lists the proxies in front of the router (an ingress, a gateway) whose X-Forwarded-For is believed, as CIDRs or single addresses. Any client can send X-Forwarded-For, so with this unset the header is ignored and client is always the TCP peer. Requires logClientAddress. The controller validates the entries. |
| `spec.policy.auth` | object |  |  |  | Auth configures request authentication. In dataPlane: Gateway mode it compiles to an Envoy AI Gateway SecurityPolicy that validates inbound JWTs and maps a verified claim onto a trusted header before any model dispatch. nil means no authentication is enforced. Authentication only; per-team model allowlists (authorization) are a separate surface. |
| `spec.policy.auth.allowlists` | array of object |  |  |  | Allowlists restricts which verified team may reach which model (authorization), as a sibling of JWT (authentication). Each entry grants a team the models it may reach. JWT proves identity; Allowlists decide what that identity is permitted to do. Has 2 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.policy.auth.jwt` | object |  |  |  | JWT enables JSON Web Token validation.
