When set (in dataPlane: Gateway mode) the gateway rejects requests without a valid token with HTTP 401 before any model dispatch, and maps the configured claim onto a trusted header. Has 5 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.policy.budgets` | array of object |  |  |  | Budgets caps token and dollar consumption per scope over a rolling window. Empty list means no budget enforcement. |
| `spec.policy.budgets[].headerKey` | string |  |  |  | HeaderKey is the request header carrying the team identifier when Scope=team. Defaults to "x-llmkube-team". |
| `spec.policy.budgets[].maxTokens` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 1 | MaxTokens caps total tokens (prompt + completion) over the window. Either MaxTokens or MaxUSD (or both) must be set. |
| `spec.policy.budgets[].maxUSD` | string |  |  | pattern `^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$` | MaxUSD caps total estimated cost in USD over the window. Cost is computed from RouterBackend.CostPerMillionTokens. |
| `spec.policy.budgets[].name` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,62}$` | Name identifies this budget for metrics, status, and audit logs. |
| `spec.policy.budgets[].ruleName` | string |  |  |  | RuleName is required when Scope=rule. References a RouterRule.Name. |
| `spec.policy.budgets[].scope` | string | yes |  | one of `"router"`, `"rule"`, `"team"` | Scope determines what the budget applies to. "router" caps all traffic through this ModelRouter. "rule" caps traffic matching a single named rule (see RuleName). "team" caps traffic identified by a request header (see HeaderKey). |
| `spec.policy.budgets[].windowSeconds` | integer (int32) |  | `3600` | min 1 | WindowSeconds is the rolling window over which the cap is evaluated. |
| `spec.policy.classification` | object |  |  |  | Classification configures how the router determines the data classification of an inbound request. |
| `spec.policy.classification.headerKey` | string |  |  |  | HeaderKey is the request header carrying the classification. Defaults to "x-llmkube-classification". |
| `spec.policy.classification.mode` | string |  | `"header-only"` | one of `"header-only"`, `"detector"`, `"hybrid"` | Mode determines how the router determines a request's classification. "header-only" (default) trusts the request header (HeaderKey, defaults to x-llmkube-classification) and never reads a classification from the request body. It is the only implemented mode. "detector" and "hybrid" are reserved for a future in-proxy content classifier (#441) and are refused today. On dataPlane: Proxy the webhook rejects them and a router that sets either is not Validated (reason ClassifierNotImplemented when nothing else in the spec is invalid). On dataPlane: Gateway and AgentGateway a dataClassification match under either is refused. |
| `spec.policy.classification.sensitiveClassifications` | array of string |  |  |  | SensitiveClassifications are the classification values that trigger fail-closed validation: any rule matching one of these values must have FailClosed=true and reference only local-tier backends. Defaults to \["pii", "phi"\]. |
| `spec.proxy` | object |  |  |  | Proxy configures the managed router-proxy Deployment (replicas, image override for air-gapped sites, resources) and the name of its Service. Sensible defaults apply when omitted. |
| `spec.proxy.affinity` | object |  |  |  | Affinity is passed through to the router-proxy pod, for node affinity or pod anti-affinity against the proxy's own replicas. The proxy pods carry the stable labels app: &lt;router&gt;-router-proxy and inference.llmkube.dev/model-router: &lt;router&gt; to select on. |
| `spec.proxy.affinity.nodeAffinity` | object |  |  |  | Describes node affinity scheduling rules for the pod. Has 21 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.proxy.affinity.podAffinity` | object |  |  |  | Describes pod affinity scheduling rules (e.g. co-locate this pod in the same node, zone, etc. as some other pod(s)). Has 36 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.proxy.affinity.podAntiAffinity` | object |  |  |  | Describes pod anti-affinity scheduling rules (e.g. avoid putting this pod in the same node, zone, etc. as some other pod(s)). Has 36 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.proxy.drainTimeout` | string |  |  |  | DrainTimeout is how long a router-proxy replica keeps serving its in-flight requests after SIGTERM, so a rollout finishes the streams it holds instead of cutting them at the proxy's own default. It sets the proxy's `--shutdown-timeout` and the pod's terminationGracePeriodSeconds, which is this value plus a fixed 5s margin so the kubelet does not SIGKILL before the drain ends. A stream longer than this still ends at the drain deadline. The proxy's default of 30s applies when unset, and nothing is rendered on the pod template, so an operator upgrade does not roll existing proxies. Changing it changes the pod template, so it rolls the proxy pods, and a replica that drains stays Terminating for up to this long, delaying that roll. Must be between 1s and 1h. |
| `spec.proxy.image` | string |  |  |  | Image overrides the default router-proxy container image. Useful for air-gapped clusters that pin to an internal registry digest. |
| `spec.proxy.imagePullSecrets` | array of object |  |  |  | ImagePullSecrets are passed through to the router-proxy pod spec. |
| `spec.proxy.imagePullSecrets[].name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.proxy.nodeSelector` | map of string |  |  |  | NodeSelector pins the router-proxy pod to nodes matching these labels. Use it when the proxy image is only present on specific nodes (for example a node-local registry) or to co-locate the proxy with its backends. Passthrough to the Pod spec. |
| `spec.proxy.podAnnotations` | map of string |  |  |  | PodAnnotations are merged into the router-proxy pod's metadata.annotations. The operator's own annotations (the config hash, and the prometheus.io scrape annotations when the operator emits them) win on collision. An annotation removed here is removed from the pods; annotations other tools add are kept. |
| `spec.proxy.podLabels` | map of string |  |  |  | PodLabels are merged into the router-proxy pod's metadata.labels, for example so label-based NetworkPolicies select the proxy. The operator's own labels (app, inference.llmkube.dev/model-router) win on collision, and the Deployment and Service selectors use only those, so changing PodLabels rolls the pods without touching a selector. A label removed here is removed from the pods; labels other tools add are kept. |
| `spec.proxy.quarantineDuration` | string |  |  |  | QuarantineDuration controls how long the proxy keeps a backend in the "skip" state after a 5xx or network error before allowing a half-open probe. A pod of an endpoint-resolution backend is quarantined after two such failures in a row, or at once when a stream goes silent past streamIdleTimeout. Default 15s when unset. Shorter windows make the proxy recover faster from transient blips; longer windows reduce probe load on genuinely-down upstreams. Tests can shrink this to sub-second to verify recovery without sleeping the full default. |
| `spec.proxy.readinessRequiredListeners` | array of integer (int32) |  |  | max items 16 | ReadinessRequiredListeners lists TCP ports that must have a LISTEN socket in the proxy pod's network namespace before the replica is Ready, for example \[15008\] for Istio ambient's HBONE inbound port, so a replica the mesh cannot reach leaves the Service endpoints. When set, the readiness probe moves to /readyz; liveness stays on /health. If the proxy cannot read /proc/net/tcp it reports Ready and logs a warning. Unset renders nothing on the pod template. |
| `spec.proxy.replicas` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 1; max 10 | Replicas is the desired number of router-proxy pods. Defaults to 1. The proxy is stateless for routing decisions; budget and SLO counters live in memory and reset on pod restart until the persistence feature lands. |
| `spec.proxy.resources` | object |  |  |  | Resources sets the pod's compute resource requests and limits. |
| `spec.proxy.resources.claims` | array of object |  |  |  | Claims lists the names of resources, defined in spec.resourceClaims, that are used by this container. Has 2 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.proxy.resources.limits` | map of int or string |  |  |  | Limits describes the maximum amount of compute resources allowed. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/` |
| `spec.proxy.resources.requests` | map of int or string |  |  |  | Requests describes the minimum amount of compute resources required. If Requests is omitted for a container, it defaults to Limits if that is explicitly specified, otherwise to an implementation-defined value. Requests cannot exceed Limits. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/` |
| `spec.proxy.responseHeaderTimeout` | string |  |  |  | ResponseHeaderTimeout caps how long the proxy waits for the upstream to begin sending response headers. For non-streaming chat completions this is effectively a max-generation-time cap, because their headers arrive when generation completes. It does not bound a streaming response once its headers arrive; StreamIdleTimeout guards those. Default 120s when unset. Per-rule and per-backend timeouts (see RouterRule.Timeout and RouterBackend.Timeout) tighten this on a per-request basis but cannot extend it beyond this cap. |
| `spec.proxy.revisionHistoryLimit` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | RevisionHistoryLimit caps how many old ReplicaSets the proxy Deployment keeps for rollback. Unset keeps 3 old ReplicaSets; 0 keeps none. Useful to bound ReplicaSet buildup, since the proxy re-rolls on every config change. |
| `spec.proxy.runtimeClassName` | string |  |  |  | RuntimeClassName selects a Kubernetes RuntimeClass for the router-proxy pod, for example a sandboxed runtime such as gVisor or Kata. Maps directly to PodSpec.RuntimeClassName. A RuntimeClass with a scheduling section has its node selector and tolerations merged into the pod by the RuntimeClass admission controller, so no separate fields are needed. Unset leaves the pod spec unchanged. |
| `spec.proxy.serviceName` | string |  |  | pattern `^[a-z]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?$`; max length 63 | ServiceName names the data-plane Service in front of the router-proxy pods, so clients can use a short in-cluster URL such as `http://llmkube.<namespace>.svc.cluster.local/v1` (with endpoint.port 80). Unset keeps the generated name, &lt;router&gt;-router-proxy. Only that Service takes this name: the Deployment, ConfigMap, admin Service (&lt;router&gt;-router-proxy-admin), pod labels, and selectors keep their generated names, so changing it does not roll the proxy pods. status.endpoint follows the Service. |
| `spec.proxy.streamIdleTimeout` | string |  |  |  | StreamIdleTimeout caps how long a response body may go without a single byte from the upstream once its headers have arrived. A healthy stream emits bytes continuously; the longest gap is the prefill before the first content token, which can take minutes for a very long prompt on slow hardware. When the window passes with no bytes, the proxy aborts the request and quarantines that backend endpoint, so a wedged replica stops receiving traffic. Default 300s when unset; "0s" disables the guard. |
| `spec.proxy.tolerations` | array of object |  |  |  | Tolerations are applied to the router-proxy pod, so it can be pinned next to tainted GPU nodes together with nodeSelector. |
| `spec.proxy.tolerations[].effect` | string |  |  |  | Effect indicates the taint effect to match. Empty means match all taint effects. When specified, allowed values are NoSchedule, PreferNoSchedule and NoExecute. |
| `spec.proxy.tolerations[].key` | string |  |  |  | Key is the taint key that the toleration applies to. Empty means match all taint keys. If the key is empty, operator must be Exists; this combination means to match all values and all keys. |
| `spec.proxy.tolerations[].operator` | string |  |  |  | Operator represents a key's relationship to the value. Valid operators are Exists, Equal, Lt, and Gt. Defaults to Equal. Exists is equivalent to wildcard for value, so that a pod can tolerate all taints of a particular category. Lt and Gt perform numeric comparisons (requires feature gate TaintTolerationComparisonOperators). |
| `spec.proxy.tolerations[].tolerationSeconds` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | TolerationSeconds represents the period of time the toleration (which must be of effect NoExecute, otherwise this field is ignored) tolerates the taint. By default, it is not set, which means tolerate the taint forever (do not evict). Zero and negative values will be treated as 0 (evict immediately) by the system. |
| `spec.proxy.tolerations[].value` | string |  |  |  | Value is the taint value the toleration matches to. If the operator is Exists, the value should be empty, otherwise just a regular string. |
| `spec.proxy.topologySpreadConstraints` | array of object |  |  |  | TopologySpreadConstraints are passed through to the router-proxy pod, for example one replica per node with topologyKey kubernetes.io/hostname and the labels above. When replicas is more than 1 and a constraint uses whenUnsatisfiable: DoNotSchedule (or the affinity requires pod anti-affinity), the proxy rolls out with maxSurge 0 and maxUnavailable 1, because a surge pod could never be placed. |
| `spec.proxy.topologySpreadConstraints[].labelSelector` | object |  |  |  | LabelSelector is used to find matching pods. Pods that match this label selector are counted to determine the number of pods in their corresponding topology domain. Has 5 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.proxy.topologySpreadConstraints[].matchLabelKeys` | array of string |  |  |  | MatchLabelKeys is a set of pod label keys to select the pods over which spreading will be calculated. The keys are used to lookup values from the incoming pod labels, those key-value labels are ANDed with labelSelector to select the group of existing pods over which spreading will be calculated for the incoming pod. The same key is forbidden to exist in both MatchLabelKeys and LabelSelector. MatchLabelKeys cannot be set when LabelSelector isn't set. Keys that don't exist in the incoming pod labels will be ignored. A null or empty list means only match against labelSelector. |
| `spec.proxy.topologySpreadConstraints[].maxSkew` | integer (int32) | yes |  |  | MaxSkew describes the degree to which pods may be unevenly distributed. When `whenUnsatisfiable=DoNotSchedule`, it is the maximum permitted difference between the number of matching pods in the target topology and the global minimum. The global minimum is the minimum number of matching pods in an eligible domain or zero if the number of eligible domains is less than MinDomains. For example, in a 3-zone cluster, MaxSkew is set to 1, and pods with the same labelSelector spread as 2/2/1: In this case, the global minimum is 1. \| zone1 \| zone2 \| zone3 \| \| P P \| P P \| P \| - if MaxSkew is 1, incoming pod can only be scheduled to zone3 to become 2/2/2; scheduling it onto zone1(zone2) would make the ActualSkew(3-1) on zone1(zone2) violate MaxSkew(1). - if MaxSkew is 2, incoming pod can be scheduled onto any zone. When `whenUnsatisfiable=ScheduleAnyway`, it is used to give higher precedence to topologies that satisfy it. It's a required field. Default value is 1 and 0 is not allowed. |
| `spec.proxy.topologySpreadConstraints[].minDomains` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | MinDomains indicates a minimum number of eligible domains. When the number of eligible domains with matching topology keys is less than minDomains, Pod Topology Spread treats "global minimum" as 0, and then the calculation of Skew is performed. And when the number of eligible domains with matching topology keys equals or greater than minDomains, this value has no effect on scheduling. As a result, when the number of eligible domains is less than minDomains, scheduler won't schedule more than maxSkew Pods to those domains. If value is nil, the constraint behaves as if MinDomains is equal to 1. Valid values are integers greater than 0. When value is not nil, WhenUnsatisfiable must be DoNotSchedule. |
| `spec.proxy.topologySpreadConstraints[].nodeAffinityPolicy` | string |  |  |  | NodeAffinityPolicy indicates how we will treat Pod's nodeAffinity/nodeSelector when calculating pod topology spread skew. Options are: - Honor: only nodes matching nodeAffinity/nodeSelector are included in the calculations. - Ignore: nodeAffinity/nodeSelector are ignored. All nodes are included in the calculations. |
| `spec.proxy.topologySpreadConstraints[].nodeTaintsPolicy` | string |  |  |  | NodeTaintsPolicy indicates how we will treat node taints when calculating pod topology spread skew. Options are: - Honor: nodes without taints, along with tainted nodes for which the incoming pod has a toleration, are included. - Ignore: node taints are ignored. All nodes are included. |
| `spec.proxy.topologySpreadConstraints[].topologyKey` | string | yes |  |  | TopologyKey is the key of node labels. Nodes that have a label with this key and identical values are considered to be in the same topology. We consider each &lt;key, value&gt; as a "bucket", and try to put balanced number of pods into each bucket. We define a domain as a particular instance of a topology. Also, we define an eligible domain as a domain whose nodes meet the requirements of nodeAffinityPolicy and nodeTaintsPolicy. e.g. If TopologyKey is "kubernetes.io/hostname", each Node is a domain of that topology. And, if TopologyKey is "topology.kubernetes.io/zone", each zone is a domain of that topology. It's a required field. |
| `spec.proxy.topologySpreadConstraints[].whenUnsatisfiable` | string | yes |  |  | WhenUnsatisfiable indicates how to deal with a pod if it doesn't satisfy the spread constraint. - DoNotSchedule (default) tells the scheduler not to schedule it. - ScheduleAnyway tells the scheduler to schedule the pod in any location, but giving higher precedence to topologies that would help reduce the skew. A constraint is considered "Unsatisfiable" for an incoming pod if and only if every possible node assignment for that pod would violate "MaxSkew" on some topology. For example, in a 3-zone cluster, MaxSkew is set to 1, and pods with the same labelSelector spread as 3/1/1: \| zone1 \| zone2 \| zone3 \| \| P P P \| P \| P \| If WhenUnsatisfiable is set to DoNotSchedule, incoming pod can only be scheduled to zone2(zone3) to become 3/2/1(3/1/2) as ActualSkew(2-1) on zone2(zone3) satisfies MaxSkew(1). In other words, the cluster can still be imbalanced, but scheduler won't make it \*more\* imbalanced. It's a required field. |
| `spec.rules` | array of object |  |  |  | Rules are evaluated in declaration order. The first matching rule wins. If no rule matches, the fallback is governed by DefaultRouteStrategy, ending at DefaultRoute. If nothing resolves, the request is rejected with HTTP 503. |
| `spec.rules[].failClosed` | boolean |  |  |  | FailClosed: when true, if no backend in Route.Backends is healthy or otherwise eligible, the router rejects the request with HTTP 503 rather than falling through to DefaultRoute or subsequent rules. This is the regulated-data gate: a fail-closed rule guarantees that matched requests are never served by any other route. |
| `spec.rules[].match` | object |  |  |  | Match groups all match conditions. All declared conditions must be true for the rule to fire (AND semantics). If Match is omitted the rule always matches (useful as a catch-all before DefaultRoute). |
| `spec.rules[].match.dataClassification` | array of string |  |  |  | DataClassification matches if the inbound request carries any of these classifications. The classification is the value of the request header named by Policy.Classification.HeaderKey (x-llmkube-classification by default); the router does not classify the request body, so a request without the header matches no dataClassification rule. Common values: "public", "internal", "confidential", "pii", "phi". |
| `spec.rules[].match.headers` | map of string |  |  |  | Headers performs exact-match equality on inbound HTTP headers (case-insensitive header name comparison). |
| `spec.rules[].match.latencySLOMs` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 1 | LatencySLOMs is a P95 first-token-latency target in milliseconds. When set, if the rolling P95 for the primary backend exceeds this value the rule promotes its declared fallback. Honored only by the "primary-fallback" strategy. |
| `spec.rules[].match.models` | array of string |  |  |  | Models matches against the OpenAI-style "model" field in the request body. Glob patterns are supported (e.g. "qwen3-\*"). |
| `spec.rules[].match.requiredCapabilities` | array of string |  |  |  | RequiredCapabilities filters backends. The rule only matches if at least one backend in Route.Backends advertises every listed capability, and on dataPlane: Proxy only those backends serve the rule's requests, under every strategy. A backend that lacks one is never tried, not even as a fallback. |
| `spec.rules[].match.taskComplexity` | string |  |  | one of `"simple"`, `"moderate"`, `"complex"` | TaskComplexity matches the inbound complexity hint (header x-llmkube-task-complexity). |
| `spec.rules[].name` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,62}$` | Name is used in audit logs and metrics labels. |
| `spec.rules[].route` | object | yes |  |  | Route is the action taken when this rule matches. |
| `spec.rules[].route.backends` | array of string | yes |  | min items 1 | Backends is an ordered list of RouterBackend.Name values. For the "primary-fallback" strategy, the first entry is the primary and subsequent entries are tried in order on failure. For "weighted", traffic is distributed across all entries by Backend.Weight. For "shadow", the first entry serves the response (falling back in order as primary-fallback does) and the entries after the one that served receive mirrored copies for evaluation only; it needs at least two. |
| `spec.rules[].route.poolActivation` | string |  | `"Wait"` | one of `"Wait"`, `"IfIdle"` | PoolActivation decides what the proxy does when a backend in Backends is a ModelPool member that is not resident and the resident member is busy. "Wait" (default) holds the request open until the incumbent drains and the target loads, bounded by the pool's swapBudget. "IfIdle" only starts a swap when the incumbent is idle; while it is busy the pooled backend is skipped immediately and the request falls through to the next entry in Backends, so a rule such as \[preferred-model, resident-model\] serves on whichever is warm instead of queueing behind in-flight work. Non-pooled backends and same-model requests are unaffected. |
| `spec.rules[].route.strategy` | string |  | `"primary-fallback"` | one of `"primary-fallback"`, `"weighted"`, `"shadow"` | Strategy selects how multiple backends are used. On dataPlane: Proxy, weighted picks a backend per request in proportion to its weight among the rule's backends that are healthy, not quarantined and advertise every required capability; a request that fails on it fails over to another backend of the rule picked the same way, never the same one twice. A weighted rule there cannot name two members of one ModelPool, since they share one GPU slot and would swap on every other request. shadow serves the client exactly as primary-fallback does and sends a copy of each request, marked x-llmkube-shadow: true, to the rule's other healthy backends after the one that served; their responses are discarded, their failures never quarantine them, and they are not charged to budgets. Copies are best effort: at most 4 run at once per backend and the rest are dropped and counted. An external backend that is a shadow target receives, and bills, every request. A shadow target cannot be a ModelPool member. dataPlane: Gateway rejects shadow. On dataPlane: AgentGateway only primary-fallback compiles, and a rule routes to its first healthy backend: when that backend's InferenceService has no ready replica, the next reconcile moves the rule to the next backend. Failover there follows replica readiness, not individual request errors. |
| `spec.rules[].timeout` | string |  |  |  | Timeout caps how long the proxy waits for the upstream to begin sending response headers on dispatches matched by this rule. When set it overrides RouterBackend.Timeout and the proxy default. Resolution order at dispatch time: rule.timeout \|\| backend.timeout \|\| proxy default. Useful for tightening regulated-data rules (sub-10s strict fail-fast) or extending long-reasoning rules (120s+). |

#### ModelRouter status

status defines the observed state of ModelRouter

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.activeRules` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | ActiveRules is the count of rules that successfully validated against current backend state. |
| `status.agentGateway` | object |  |  |  | AgentGateway reports the observed state of dataPlane: AgentGateway exposure: whether the InferencePool / HTTPRoute / model-extraction AgentgatewayPolicy reconciled against the referenced agentgateway Gateway, and the resolved endpoint. nil in Proxy and Gateway modes. Also surfaced via the AgentGatewayReady condition. |
| `status.agentGateway.endpoint` | string |  |  |  | Endpoint is the agentgateway address clients send OpenAI requests to, resolved from the referenced Gateway. Empty until the pool is ready. |
| `status.agentGateway.poolReady` | boolean |  |  |  | PoolReady indicates the generated InferencePool(s) reconciled against the referenced agentgateway Gateway. |
| `status.backends` | array of object |  |  |  | Backends reports the resolved address and current health of every declared backend. |
| `status.backends[].address` | string |  |  |  | Address is the resolved upstream URL the router-proxy dispatches to. For local backends this is the InferenceService's cluster URL; for external backends it is the provider's base URL. |
| `status.backends[].healthy` | boolean |  |  |  | Healthy reflects the most recent probe result. |
| `status.backends[].lastProbeTime` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastProbeTime is when the proxy last completed a health probe for this backend. |
| `status.backends[].message` | string |  |  |  | Message provides extra context, especially when Healthy is false (e.g. "InferenceService not Ready", "Secret missing key ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"). |
| `status.backends[].name` | string | yes |  |  | Name matches RouterBackend.Name. |
| `status.backends[].tier` | string |  |  |  | Tier mirrors RouterBackend.Tier for convenience. |
| `status.budgetUtilization` | array of object |  |  |  | BudgetUtilization summarises current budget consumption. |
| `status.budgetUtilization[].name` | string | yes |  |  | Name matches BudgetSpec.Name. |
| `status.budgetUtilization[].tokensUsed` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | TokensUsed is the rolling-window token count. |
| `status.budgetUtilization[].usdUsed` | string |  |  |  | USDUsed is the rolling-window estimated cost in USD. |
| `status.budgetUtilization[].utilization` | string |  |  |  | Utilization is the fraction of the budget consumed, 0.0 to 1.0. When both MaxTokens and MaxUSD are set this is the maximum of the two utilizations. |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | conditions represent the current state of the ModelRouter resource. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.endpoint` | string |  |  |  | Endpoint is the in-cluster URL clients should hit. Populated once the router-proxy Service is ready. |
| `status.gateway` | object |  |  |  | Gateway reports the observed state of dataPlane: Gateway exposure: whether the AIGatewayRoute (and its backing Backend / AIServiceBackend / BackendTrafficPolicy) reconciled, and the resolved gateway endpoint. nil in Proxy mode. Also surfaced via the GatewayReady condition. |
| `status.gateway.authEnabled` | boolean |  |  |  | AuthEnabled indicates a SecurityPolicy enforcing JWT authentication was compiled for this route (ModelRouter policy.auth.jwt). Set by the ModelRouter dataPlane: Gateway path; false when no auth is configured. |
| `status.gateway.endpoint` | string |  |  |  | Endpoint is the gateway address clients send OpenAI requests to. Set by the ModelRouter dataPlane: Gateway path (resolved from the referenced Gateway); empty for the InferenceService path. |
| `status.gateway.modelName` | string |  |  |  | ModelName is the resolved model-name match value clients send as the OpenAI "model" string to reach this InferenceService through the gateway. Set by the InferenceService path; empty for ModelRouter (which fronts many model names). |
| `status.gateway.routeReady` | boolean |  |  |  | RouteReady indicates the AIGatewayRoute (and its backing Backend + AIServiceBackend) were reconciled successfully against the gateway. |
| `status.lastUpdated` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastUpdated is the timestamp of the last status reconciliation. |
| `status.phase` | string |  |  | one of `"Pending"`, `"Provisioning"`, `"Ready"`, `"Degraded"`, `"Failed"` | Phase is a coarse summary of the router's state. Possible values: Pending, Provisioning, Ready, Degraded, Failed. |

## foreman.llmkube.dev

### Agent

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `Agent` |
| API versions | `foreman.llmkube.dev/v1alpha1` |
| Scope | Namespaced |
| Plural | `agents` |
| Short names | `ag` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_agents.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_agents.yaml) |

#### Agent printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Role | `string` | `.spec.role` | 0 |  |
| Model | `string` | `.spec.model` | 0 |  |
| InferenceService | `string` | `.spec.inferenceServiceRef.name` | 0 |  |
| Validated | `string` | `.status.conditions[?(@.type=="Validated")].status` | 0 |  |
| Age | `date` | `.metadata.creationTimestamp` | 0 |  |

#### Agent spec

spec is the role definition.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.bashTimeoutSeconds` | integer (int32) |  | `30` | min 1 | BashTimeoutSeconds bounds a single bash tool invocation. The bash tool runs under "sh -c" with cwd pinned to the workspace and a scrubbed environment; this cap stops a runaway test or grep from stalling the whole agent loop. |
| `spec.chatTemplateKwargs` | map of any |  |  |  | ChatTemplateKwargs is a free-form map of template-specific keyword arguments forwarded verbatim on every chat-completions request as chat_template_kwargs. Each value is a raw JSON blob so booleans stay booleans (a JSON false must not become the string "false"). The primary use case is disabling thinking on reasoning models: llama.cpp supplies enable_thinking=true itself when the client omits the kwarg, so every request today is thinking-on. Setting `{"enable_thinking": false}` here turns it off. |
| `spec.contextStrategy` | string |  | `"window"` | one of `"window"`, `"session"` | ContextStrategy selects how the loop builds each request's message list. "window" (default) applies observation masking bounded by ObservationWindowTurns: older tool results are masked, keeping the payload small for small-context models but rewriting the prefix each turn (which defeats prompt caching). "session" keeps a stable, append-only prefix so a caching runtime reuses it across turns, and compacts (drops the oldest middle turns, pinning the system prompt, the task, and the most recent turn) only when the payload approaches ContextWindowTokens. Use "session" for large-context models on caching runtimes; set ContextHardCap &gt;= the server context size so a healthy deep session is not aborted early. See issue #756. |
| `spec.contextWindowTokens` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | ContextWindowTokens is the soft token budget for the wire payload the loop sends on every turn. When the running message list approximately exceeds this size, older tool result messages are masked to a one-line header until the budget is met. The persisted transcript ConfigMap still captures the FULL (unmasked) trajectory for review. Zero uses the executor default (32768 tokens). |
| `spec.execution` | object |  |  |  | Execution selects where the agent loop runs. Omitted = InProcess. |
| `spec.execution.activeDeadlineSeconds` | integer (int64) |  | `3600` |  | ActiveDeadlineSeconds bounds the Job's wall-clock runtime. |
| `spec.execution.image` | string |  |  |  | Image is the per-task Job image: the foreman-agent binary plus the project toolchain (go/make/git). Required when Mode is Job. |
| `spec.execution.mode` | string |  | `"InProcess"` | one of `"InProcess"`, `"Job"` | Mode selects the execution strategy. Default InProcess. |
| `spec.execution.resources` | object |  |  |  | Resources overrides the Job container resources (defaults mirror the gate Job). |
| `spec.execution.resources.claims` | array of object |  |  |  | Claims lists the names of resources, defined in spec.resourceClaims, that are used by this container. Has 2 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.execution.resources.limits` | map of int or string |  |  |  | Limits describes the maximum amount of compute resources allowed. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/` |
| `spec.execution.resources.requests` | map of int or string |  |  |  | Requests describes the minimum amount of compute resources required. If Requests is omitted for a container, it defaults to Limits if that is explicitly specified, otherwise to an implementation-defined value. Requests cannot exceed Limits. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/` |
| `spec.execution.serviceAccountName` | string |  |  |  | ServiceAccountName runs the Job pod under a least-privilege SA. |
| `spec.inferenceServiceRef` | object |  |  |  | InferenceServiceRef names the LLMKube InferenceService in the same namespace that serves this Agent's model. The foreman-agent resolves it to a base URL ("`http://<svc>.<ns>.svc:<port>/v1`") at task time. v0.1 uses inferenceServiceRef exclusively; v0.2 may introduce an external-provider URL form. |
| `spec.inferenceServiceRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.maxConcurrentTasks` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 1 | MaxConcurrentTasks bounds how many of this Agent's tasks may be in flight at once. When set, the AgenticTask controller does not claim an (N+1)th task for this Agent until one of its in-flight tasks completes; excess tasks stay Pending for the next pass. Unset means unbounded (today's behavior). |
| `spec.maxEnvtestIterations` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MaxEnvtestIterations bounds how many times the executor re-runs a coder after the post-push envtest gate fails, feeding the gate output back so the coder fixes the failure and the branch is re-gated (#768). Three-valued: nil defaults to 1; an explicit 0 restores the pre-#768 behavior (a failing gate downgrades to INCOMPLETE on the first failure); N allows up to N retries before the downgrade. Applies only to coder issue-fix tasks whose change touches an envtest-backed package and whose gate runner is wired. |
| `spec.maxOutputTokens` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MaxOutputTokens caps the tokens the model may generate per turn (the OAI max_tokens field). Reasoning models (qwen3-family served with `--reasoning-parser`) engage heavy &lt;think&gt; only on decision turns; a decision turn needs enough budget to finish reasoning AND emit the tool call. With no cap, generation budget defaults to the server's remaining context (max_model_len - prompt), so on a large-context serve a decision turn can reason effectively unbounded for hours before it acts or hits the context limit, which reads as a stalled task. Set this high enough to complete a &lt;think&gt; plus its tool call, but well below the served context so vLLM does not reject the request when prompt + maxOutputTokens exceeds max_model_len. Zero uses the executor default (8192 tokens). |
| `spec.maxRetries` | integer (int32) |  | `3` | min 0; max 20 | MaxRetries bounds how many times the loop retries a single turn on recoverable errors (notably llama.cpp #22072 truncated tool_call argument JSON). Bounded exponential backoff with jitter. |
| `spec.maxScanIterations` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MaxScanIterations bounds how many times the executor re-runs a coder after the post-push container-image scan gate fails, feeding the scan findings back so the coder fixes them and the branch is re-scanned. Three-valued: nil defaults to 1; an explicit 0 downgrades a failing scan on the first failure; N allows up to N retries. Applies only to coder issue-fix tasks that declare spec.scanGate and whose scan runner is wired. |
| `spec.maxTurns` | integer (int32) |  | `50` | min 1; max 500 | MaxTurns caps the agent loop's iterations before it gives up. Each turn is one chat-completions call plus its tool dispatches. |
| `spec.mcp` | object |  |  |  | MCP configures the agent's MCP (Model Context Protocol) client. Nil or Enabled=false runs the agent with no MCP tools (v0.1 behavior, unchanged). |
| `spec.mcp.callTimeout` | string |  |  |  | CallTimeout bounds a single MCP tool call. Defaults to 30s when zero. |
| `spec.mcp.enabled` | boolean |  |  |  | Enabled turns the MCP client on for this agent (opt-in; default off). |
| `spec.mcp.maxResultBytes` | integer |  |  |  | MaxResultBytes caps an MCP tool result before truncation. Defaults to 32768 when zero. |
| `spec.mcp.servers` | array of object |  |  |  | Servers is the set of MCP servers to connect to. |
| `spec.mcp.servers[].allowedTools` | array of string |  |  |  | AllowedTools whitelists tool names this server may expose ("\*" or empty = all). |
| `spec.mcp.servers[].headers` | array of object |  |  |  | Headers are extra HTTP headers (e.g. auth), sourced from secrets. Has 5 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.mcp.servers[].name` | string | yes |  |  | Name namespaces this server's tools as mcp\_\_&lt;name&gt;\_\_&lt;tool&gt;. |
| `spec.mcp.servers[].transport` | string | yes |  | one of `"http"` | Transport is the MCP transport. Phase 1 supports "http" only. |
| `spec.mcp.servers[].url` | string | yes |  |  | URL is the MCP server endpoint (streamable HTTP). |
| `spec.model` | string |  |  |  | Model is a free-form identifier for the model this Agent expects the referenced InferenceService to serve. Cosmetic in v0.1 (the runtime endpoint comes from InferenceServiceRef); v0.2 will use it to validate the InferenceService is actually serving the expected model before the agent loop dispatches. |
| `spec.modelProfileRef` | string |  |  |  | ModelProfileRef names a cluster-scoped ModelProfile whose tuning (system-prompt addendum, stuck-loop overrides, forcing-phase read restriction) is layered onto this Agent at dispatch. Empty = none. The profile is additive: it appends to SystemPrompt and overrides stuck-loop knobs only when this Agent does not set its own stuckLoopDetection. |
| `spec.observationWindowTurns` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0; max 50 | ObservationWindowTurns is the number of most-recent tool result messages kept in full before older ones are masked, regardless of the token budget. Acts as a floor (the model always sees this many recent tool outputs verbatim). Zero uses the executor default (3). |
| `spec.openPullRequestsAsDraft` | boolean |  |  |  | OpenPullRequestsAsDraft controls whether a pull request this agent opens is created as a draft (#1706). Nil means true, preserving the behaviour introduced in #1703. |
| `spec.provider` | string |  | `"local"` | one of `"local"`, `"cloud-proxy"`, `"anthropic"` | Provider selects the model-serving backend. Default "local" keeps the v0.1 behavior (resolve InferenceServiceRef). v0.2 adds "cloud-proxy" for OpenAI-compatible HTTP endpoints (typically a LiteLLM gateway); see ProviderConfig. Cloud-proxy Agents are subject to the foreman-operator's `--allow-cloud-providers` kill switch and to per-Workload spec.allowCloudReviewers gating. |
| `spec.providerConfig` | object |  |  |  | ProviderConfig configures non-local providers. Required when Provider is "cloud-proxy"; ignored for "local". |
| `spec.providerConfig.apiKeySecretRef` | object |  |  |  | APIKeySecretRef references a Secret carrying the credential the executor sends with every request. The header is provider-shaped: cloud-proxy sends it as Authorization: Bearer &lt;value&gt; (the LiteLLM gateway contract); anthropic sends it verbatim as the x-api-key header (the Messages API contract). Optional: when nil, the endpoint is dialed without auth (LAN-only proxies behind a network policy are a common case). |
| `spec.providerConfig.apiKeySecretRef.key` | string | yes |  |  | The key of the secret to select from. Must be a valid secret key. |
| `spec.providerConfig.apiKeySecretRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.providerConfig.apiKeySecretRef.optional` | boolean |  |  |  | Specify whether the Secret or its key must be defined |
| `spec.providerConfig.baseURL` | string |  |  | min length 1 | BaseURL is the HTTP endpoint the executor dispatches to. The path appended depends on the provider: cloud-proxy posts to /chat/completions (supply the /v1 prefix, e.g. "`http://foundation-router.lan:4000/v1`"); anthropic posts to /messages (supply the /v1 prefix, e.g. "`https://api.anthropic.com/v1`"). Required for non-local providers. |
| `spec.providerConfig.model` | string |  |  | min length 1 | Model is the identifier the upstream expects in the request body (e.g. "claude-sonnet-4-6", "gpt-4o", "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6" when LiteLLM is in front). Required for non-local providers; overrides AgentSpec.Model on the wire while AgentSpec.Model remains the human-readable handle. |
| `spec.requestTimeoutSeconds` | integer (int32) |  | `3600` | min 1 | RequestTimeoutSeconds is the loop-wide wall-clock budget for the whole agent run (#532). When the budget is exhausted mid-turn the loop exits gracefully with an INCOMPLETE verdict and the partial transcript preserved, rather than failing the task with a retry-less ExecutorError. This is distinct from the per-request header timeout (RequestTurnTimeoutSeconds): a single slow turn no longer caps the whole loop, and vice versa. |
| `spec.requestTurnTimeoutSeconds` | integer (int32) |  | `120` | min 1 | RequestTurnTimeoutSeconds bounds a single chat-completions HTTP request's "awaiting response headers" wait, i.e. how long the agent waits for the first token of one turn before treating the request as a transient timeout and retrying it (up to MaxRetries). Keep this tight relative to RequestTimeoutSeconds so a slow warm-context turn fails fast and retries instead of hanging out the whole loop budget. |
| `spec.requiredCapability` | object |  |  |  | RequiredCapability filters which FleetNodes can serve this Agent. When an AgenticTask references this Agent via spec.agentRef, the scheduler uses this RequiredCapability and ignores the task's own spec.requiredCapability. Single source of truth. |
| `spec.requiredCapability.accelerator` | string |  | `"any"` | one of `"metal"`, `"cuda"`, `"vulkan"`, `"any"` | Accelerator selects an accelerator family. "any" matches any node. |
| `spec.requiredCapability.minContextTokens` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MinContextTokens is the minimum context window the node's installed model must support. Set to 0 to leave unconstrained. |
| `spec.requiredCapability.minRAMGB` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MinRAMGB is the minimum available RAM the node must advertise. |
| `spec.requiredCapability.nodeSelector` | map of string |  |  |  | NodeSelector is a hard pin: only FleetNodes whose labels match every key are eligible. Used for tasks that must run on a specific node (e.g. verify tasks targeting the gate runner). |
| `spec.requiredCapability.requiresModelInstalled` | boolean |  |  |  | RequiresModelInstalled, when true, scopes scheduling to FleetNodes that already have this Agent's model resident (the model name, resolved from Agent.spec.model or Agent.spec.inferenceServiceRef, must appear in the node's status.capability.installedModels). In that mode the minRAMGB gate is intentionally ignored: the model is already loaded, so the agent loop needs ~0 additional RAM. This is the warm-driver path reviewer-class Agents always take; minRAMGB is correct only for the cold-load path. When false (default), minRAMGB is enforced as before. |
| `spec.requiredCapability.roles` | array of string |  |  |  | Roles filters to FleetNodes whose spec.roles include every named role. Matches against FleetNodeSpec.Roles (the `--roles` flag on the foreman-agent). Used to route the gate Agent's tasks to nodes that advertise themselves as verifiers without coupling to per-node labels. |
| `spec.role` | string | yes |  | one of `"coder"`, `"verifier"`, `"reviewer"`, `"planner"` | Role discriminates the pipeline step this Agent fulfills. Currently informational (used for observability + future learning routing); the scheduler does not branch on it in v0.1. |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection` | object |  |  |  | StuckLoopDetection configures the per-Agent stuck-loop detector (#544). When nil the executor applies a conservative default (5 repeated calls / 15 edit-free turns / 90k soft cap / 140k hard cap). Set fields to zero to disable a specific signal; set the whole pointer to a struct with all-zero fields to disable the detector entirely for this Agent (useful for review-only roles where re-reading the same diff is normal). |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection.contextHardCap` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | ContextHardCap is the approximate-token budget that force- terminates without a nudge stage. Must be &gt; ContextSoftCap when both are set; the executor disables both if soft &gt;= hard rather than producing inconsistent decisions. |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection.contextSoftCap` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | ContextSoftCap is the approximate-token budget that nudges the model once. Zero disables (paired with ContextHardCap). |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection.editFreeTurnsLimit` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0; max 200 | EditFreeTurnsLimit is the number of consecutive turns without a write_file, str_replace, or submit_result call required to fire the edit-free signal. Zero disables. |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection.repeatedToolThreshold` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0; max 100 | RepeatedToolThreshold is the number of identical (tool_name, args) calls required to fire the duplicate-call signal. Zero disables. |
| `spec.systemPrompt` | string |  |  |  | SystemPrompt is the literal system message the Agent sees on every run. Inline (no ConfigMap indirection) for v0.1; ConfigMap indirection is a non-breaking v0.2 addition. |
| `spec.temperature` | string |  |  | pattern `^[0-2](\.[0-9]+)?$` | Temperature is the sampling temperature passed verbatim on each chat-completions request. String-typed to dodge float-on-CRD complications and to allow exact roundtrip; parsed as a float at loop start. Expected range "0.0" through "2.0"; the loop rejects anything outside. |
| `spec.tools` | array of string | yes |  | min items 1 | Tools is the tool whitelist surfaced to the model on every turn. Unknown names are rejected at agent startup so a typo in an Agent CR fails loud rather than silently disabling a tool. |
| `spec.vision` | object |  |  |  | Vision opts this Agent into multimodal input, so images attached to a task payload are sent to the model. See VisionSpec. |
| `spec.vision.enabled` | boolean |  |  |  | Enabled turns on image attachment for this Agent. |
| `spec.vision.maxImageBytes` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | MaxImageBytes caps each image AFTER base64 encoding, which inflates by about a third. Defaults to 4MiB when unset. A 947KB screenshot became 484KB of base64 in practice, so a handful of unbounded images would consume a context window whole. |
| `spec.vision.maxImages` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | MaxImages caps how many images one message may carry. Defaults to 4. |

#### Agent status

status is the reconciler's observed view. M3 reconciler is a stub and does not write to it; status fields are reserved for v0.2.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | Conditions track standard signals. M3 reserves "Ready" and "Validated"; the reconciler does not write either in M3. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | ObservedGeneration is the most recent .metadata.generation the reconciler has processed. Lets clients tell at a glance whether a spec edit has been observed yet. |

### AgentRelease

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `AgentRelease` |
| API versions | `foreman.llmkube.dev/v1alpha1` |
| Scope | Cluster |
| Plural | `agentreleases` |
| Short names | `agr` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_agentreleases.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_agentreleases.yaml) |

#### AgentRelease printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Phase | `string` | `.status.phase` | 0 |  |
| Version | `string` | `.spec.version` | 0 |  |
| Approved | `boolean` | `.spec.approved` | 0 |  |
| Updated | `integer` | `.status.updatedNodes` | 0 |  |
| Age | `date` | `.metadata.creationTimestamp` | 0 |  |

#### AgentRelease spec

spec describes the desired rollout configuration.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.agentKind` | string | yes |  | one of `"foreman-agent"`, `"metal-agent"` | AgentKind identifies which agent binary this release targets. The controller uses this to filter FleetNodes by their reported AgentKind. |
| `spec.approved` | boolean |  | `false` |  | Approved is the human-approval gate. When false (the default) the controller waits in AwaitingApproval before beginning any rollout. Set to true to allow the rollout to proceed. |
| `spec.artifacts` | array of object |  |  |  | Artifacts is the list of platform-specific binaries for this release. The controller selects the appropriate artifact for each FleetNode based on its reported OS and architecture. |
| `spec.artifacts[].arch` | string | yes |  | one of `"amd64"`, `"arm64"` | Arch is the target CPU architecture for this artifact. |
| `spec.artifacts[].os` | string | yes |  | one of `"darwin"`, `"linux"` | OS is the target operating system for this artifact. |
| `spec.artifacts[].sha256` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[0-9a-f]{64}$` | SHA256 is the lowercase hex-encoded SHA-256 digest of the artifact binary. This is the trust anchor: the operator verifies the download against this digest before dispatching the update to any node. |
| `spec.artifacts[].url` | string | yes |  |  | URL is the download location for this artifact. The operator will fetch it on behalf of target nodes unless a pre-placement mechanism is in use. |
| `spec.concurrency` | integer (int32) |  | `1` | min 1 | Concurrency is the maximum number of nodes that may be updating simultaneously. Defaults to 1 (one node at a time). |
| `spec.healthGate` | object |  |  |  | HealthGate configures the post-update health check parameters applied after each node is updated. |
| `spec.healthGate.minHealthySeconds` | integer (int32) |  | `60` | min 0 | MinHealthySeconds is how long a freshly-updated node must report a fresh heartbeat continuously before the controller considers it healthy. Defaults to 60 seconds. |
| `spec.healthGate.timeoutSeconds` | integer (int32) |  | `600` | min 1 | TimeoutSeconds is the maximum time the controller waits for a node to pass the health gate before marking the node Failed. Defaults to 600 seconds (10 minutes). |
| `spec.paused` | boolean |  | `false` |  | Paused suspends all further rollout activity when true. Nodes that are already Updating continue to completion; no new nodes are picked up. |
| `spec.selector` | object |  |  |  | Selector restricts the rollout to FleetNodes whose labels match. When nil, all FleetNodes of the given AgentKind are targeted. |
| `spec.selector.matchExpressions` | array of object |  |  |  | matchExpressions is a list of label selector requirements. The requirements are ANDed. |
| `spec.selector.matchExpressions[].key` | string | yes |  |  | key is the label key that the selector applies to. |
| `spec.selector.matchExpressions[].operator` | string | yes |  |  | operator represents a key's relationship to a set of values. Valid operators are In, NotIn, Exists and DoesNotExist. |
| `spec.selector.matchExpressions[].values` | array of string |  |  |  | values is an array of string values. If the operator is In or NotIn, the values array must be non-empty. If the operator is Exists or DoesNotExist, the values array must be empty. This array is replaced during a strategic merge patch. |
| `spec.selector.matchLabels` | map of string |  |  |  | matchLabels is a map of `{key,value}` pairs. A single `{key,value}` in the matchLabels map is equivalent to an element of matchExpressions, whose key field is "key", the operator is "In", and the values array contains only "value". The requirements are ANDed. |
| `spec.version` | string | yes |  |  | Version is the target agent version string (e.g. "v0.9.0"). The controller matches this against FleetNode.status.agentVersion to determine which nodes require an update. |

#### AgentRelease status

status is the controller's observed rollout state. Do not edit manually.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | Conditions is the set of standard Kubernetes conditions for this release. The controller uses the types Approved, Progressing, and Complete. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.desiredVersion` | string |  |  |  | DesiredVersion mirrors spec.version at the time of last reconciliation, allowing status readers to identify version drift without reading spec. |
| `status.inFlightNodes` | array of string |  |  |  | InFlightNodes lists the names of FleetNodes that are currently being updated (Phase=Updating). The length of this list is bounded by spec.concurrency. |
| `status.nodeStatuses` | array of object |  |  |  | NodeStatuses is the per-node breakdown of rollout progress. |
| `status.nodeStatuses[].dispatchedAt` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | DispatchedAt is the time the controller wrote the UpdateRequest onto the FleetNode. The timeout clock (HealthGate.TimeoutSeconds) is measured from this timestamp. Set at dispatch; not updated thereafter. |
| `status.nodeStatuses[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastTransitionTime is the time the node first reported the target agent version (i.e. the soak clock start). The health-gate soak window (HealthGate.MinHealthySeconds) is measured from this timestamp. |
| `status.nodeStatuses[].message` | string |  |  |  | Message is a human-readable reason for the current Phase (particularly useful when Phase is Failed). |
| `status.nodeStatuses[].name` | string | yes |  |  | Name is the FleetNode name (metadata.name of the FleetNode CR). |
| `status.nodeStatuses[].observedVersion` | string |  |  |  | ObservedVersion is the agent version last reported by this node's FleetNode heartbeat. Empty until the node starts reporting version information. |
| `status.nodeStatuses[].phase` | string |  |  | one of `"Pending"`, `"Updating"`, `"Updated"`, `"Failed"` | Phase is this node's position in the update lifecycle. |
| `status.nodeStatuses[].reason` | string |  |  | one of `"NodeUpdateTimeout"`, `"NoMatchingArtifact"` | Reason is a machine-readable explanation for why Phase is Failed. Set only when Phase==Failed; empty otherwise. |
| `status.observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | ObservedGeneration is the metadata.generation of the AgentRelease spec that was most recently reconciled. Used to detect pending spec changes. |
| `status.phase` | string |  |  | one of `"Pending"`, `"AwaitingApproval"`, `"InProgress"`, `"Paused"`, `"Succeeded"`, `"Failed"` | Phase is the overall rollout lifecycle state. |
| `status.targetNodes` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | TargetNodes is the total number of FleetNodes that match this release's selector and AgentKind. |
| `status.updatedNodes` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | UpdatedNodes is the count of nodes that have been successfully updated and passed the health gate. |

### AgenticTask

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `AgenticTask` |
| API versions | `foreman.llmkube.dev/v1alpha1` |
| Scope | Namespaced |
| Plural | `agentictasks` |
| Short names | `at` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_agentictasks.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_agentictasks.yaml) |

#### AgenticTask printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Phase | `string` | `.status.phase` | 0 |  |
| Kind | `string` | `.spec.kind` | 0 |  |
| Node | `string` | `.status.assignedNode` | 0 |  |
| Verdict | `string` | `.status.verdict` | 0 |  |
| Age | `date` | `.metadata.creationTimestamp` | 0 |  |

#### AgenticTask spec

spec is the desired task definition.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.agentRef` | object |  |  |  | AgentRef references an Agent (in the same namespace) that runs this step. When set, the scheduler resolves the Agent and uses Agent.spec.requiredCapability for capability matching, ignoring this task's own RequiredCapability. Empty preserves the M2 path in which the task's RequiredCapability is authoritative. |
| `spec.agentRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.dependsOn` | array of string |  |  |  | DependsOn lists AgenticTasks (by name in the same namespace) that must reach Succeeded before this task is dispatched. v0.1 uses this only to chain verify tasks behind their parent issue-fix. |
| `spec.gateProfile` | object |  |  |  | GateProfile declares the gate commands, container image, and source file extensions for the task's language. Consumed in a later slice by the gate executor; unset means the "go" preset (current behavior). |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands` | object |  |  |  | Commands overrides the preset commands. Only non-empty fields replace the preset; empty fields keep the preset value. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.build` | string |  |  |  | Build is the command that compiles the project. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.codegenCheck` | string |  |  |  | CodegenCheck is the command that verifies generated files are in sync with their sources. Empty means no codegen check. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.format` | string |  |  |  | Format is the command that checks source formatting. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.lint` | string |  |  |  | Lint is the command that runs the linter. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.test` | string |  |  |  | Test is the command that runs the test suite. |
| `spec.gateProfile.image` | string |  |  |  | Image is the container image used to run the gate checks. |
| `spec.gateProfile.language` | string |  |  | one of `"go"`, `"python"`, `"rust"`, `"node"`, `"generic"` | Language selects the built-in preset. Defaults to "go" when empty. |
| `spec.gateProfile.sourceExtensions` | array of string |  |  |  | SourceExtensions lists the file extensions the gate should consider as source files (e.g. ".go", ".py"). |
| `spec.gateProfile.testLayout` | object |  |  |  | TestLayout declares where this repository's tests live relative to the code they cover, so the reviewer's scope-overlap check can map a test file back to the module it covers. Leave unset when tests sit beside the code, which is the common case and the built-in behaviour. |
| `spec.gateProfile.testLayout.sourceRoot` | string |  |  |  | SourceRoot is the directory the code under test lives under, e.g. "src/main/java" for Maven/Gradle, or "lib" for Ruby. |
| `spec.gateProfile.testLayout.testRoot` | string |  |  |  | TestRoot is the directory tests live under, e.g. "src/test/java" for Maven/Gradle, or "spec" for a Ruby project. |
| `spec.kind` | string | yes |  | one of `"issue-fix"`, `"verify"`, `"review"`, `"freeform"`, `"integrate"`, `"reconcile"` | Kind selects the work type and payload shape. |
| `spec.mcpEnabled` | boolean |  |  |  | MCPEnabled is the reconciler-propagated effective Workload opt-out that reaches the executor without a live Workload GET. Three-valued via the \*bool: nil or true means MCP is allowed (subject to the referenced Agent's own spec.mcp config); false disables MCP for this run, for benchmark control runs that must be comparable to a no-MCP baseline. The WorkloadReconciler stamps this from Workload.spec.mcpEnabled onto every child task it emits; hand- authored AgenticTasks may set it directly. |
| `spec.modelRef` | string |  |  |  | ModelRef names the Model the agent should use. Optional; the scheduler can pick a default based on RequiredCapability. |
| `spec.payload` | object | yes |  |  | Payload is the kind-discriminated work spec. |
| `spec.payload.agent` | string |  |  |  | Agent is the named agent to invoke. Required for freeform; defaults to "issue-fixer" for issue-fix and "verify" for verify. |
| `spec.payload.allowOverwrite` | boolean |  |  |  | AllowOverwrite permits the coder to replace a stale remote ref for its own branch (force-with-lease pinned to the observed SHA) when a plain push is rejected non-fast-forward. Opt-in, per the design decision in #573: silently overwriting published branches is the wrong default for a system that leaves an audit trail, but automated retry systems that delete-and-recreate Workloads derive the same branch name every run and are otherwise permanently wedged by their predecessor's ref (#934). Stamped from Workload.spec.allowOverwrite in issue-batch mode; explicit pipelines set it per step. |
| `spec.payload.baseBranch` | string |  |  |  | BaseBranch is the upstream base ref the coder branch is cut from (and the verify gate's bite check diffs against). On an issue-fix task the executor fetches this ref from the upstream repo (payload.repo) and branches off it, so a stale fork default branch does not produce a stale-base branch. Optional; defaults to "main". |
| `spec.payload.branch` | string |  |  |  | Branch is the existing branch to gate. Required for verify. |
| `spec.payload.branchPrefix` | string |  |  |  | BranchPrefix overrides the branch name prefix on issue-fix tasks (default derived from the issue's labels via conventional commit prefixes: fix/, feat/, chore/, etc.). |
| `spec.payload.branchStrategy` | string |  | `"reset"` | one of `"reset"`, `"rebase"` | BranchStrategy selects how the working branch is cut relative to the current base (see BranchStrategy). Defaults to "reset": cut fresh from the current base and ignore any prior attempt (reviseFromBranch), so a retry or repair re-dispatch cannot revert merged work. Set "rebase" for the in-review PR revision path, where the prior attempt is restored and rebased onto the current base. Issue-fix only. |
| `spec.payload.contract` | string |  |  |  | Contract is the slice plan's prose contract, handed to a reconcile task's LLM sweep as the description of the intended shared interfaces. Reconcile only. Part of #1033. |
| `spec.payload.gateAdvisories` | array of object |  |  |  | GateAdvisories holds non-blocking findings copied from the upstream coder task's result, surfaced to a reviewer for confirmation or dismissal. The WorkloadReconciler populates this field on review-kind tasks once the coder task has a terminal result with advisories in status.result.extra\["gateAdvisories"\]. Empty when the coder task produced no advisories or has not yet completed. |
| `spec.payload.gateAdvisories[].check` | string |  |  |  | Check is the name of the gate check that produced the finding (e.g. "grounding-breadth", "scope-overlap"). |
| `spec.payload.gateAdvisories[].detail` | string |  |  |  | Detail is the human-readable description of the advisory (e.g. "cites dcgm_gpu_utilization (unknown symbol)"). |
| `spec.payload.images` | array of string |  |  |  | Images are workspace-relative paths to images attached to the first user message, for agents whose model can accept them (Agent.spec.vision.enabled). The intended use is showing a coder what its own change looks like: a screenshot of a rendered page alongside the prompt describing what is wrong with it. |
| `spec.payload.issue` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 1 | Issue is the GitHub issue number. Required for issue-fix. |
| `spec.payload.openPullRequest` | boolean |  |  |  | OpenPullRequest instructs the reviewer-role executor to open a pull request for the task's branch when its review verdict is GO (idempotent: an existing PR for the branch is reused). Stamped from Workload.spec.openPullRequest onto review steps in issue-batch mode; explicit pipelines set it per step. The PR is the Workload's artifact — without this the pipeline ends at a reviewed-but-unopened branch (#937). |
| `spec.payload.prompt` | string |  |  |  | Prompt is the agent input. Required for freeform. |
| `spec.payload.promptPrefix` | string |  |  |  | PromptPrefix is prepended to the assembled user prompt for an issue-fix task, BEFORE the fetched issue body. Unlike Prompt (which, when set, suppresses the issue-body fetch), PromptPrefix leaves the issue-body fetch intact, so the agent sees both this hint and the issue's acceptance criteria. Used by the coder escalation tier to carry the prior model's diagnosis. |
| `spec.payload.repo` | string |  |  |  | Repo is the "owner/name" GitHub repo. Required for issue-fix and verify. |
| `spec.payload.reviseFromBranch` | string |  |  |  | ReviseFromBranch names a ref on the push remote that the executor cuts the working branch FROM instead of from BaseBranch, so a revision task starts with its prior attempt's files present (#951). Issue-fix only. The WorkloadReconciler stamps this on fix- iteration coder tasks (the prior attempt lives at the task's own branch name on the push remote). The executor owns the git restore — no prompt-driven fetch/checkout. If the ref does not exist on the remote (e.g. pruned, or the prior attempt never pushed), the executor logs and falls back to branching from BaseBranch rather than failing. |
| `spec.payload.sharedIdentifiers` | array of object |  |  |  | SharedIdentifiers pins the exact cross-slice strings a reconcile task verifies against the integrated union. Reconcile only. Part of #1033. |
| `spec.payload.sharedIdentifiers[].definedBy` | string | yes |  | min length 1 | DefinedBy is the slice name that must produce the identifier. |
| `spec.payload.sharedIdentifiers[].id` | string | yes |  | min length 1 | ID is the exact string every listed slice must contain verbatim. |
| `spec.payload.sharedIdentifiers[].referencedBy` | array of string |  |  |  | ReferencedBy are the slice names that must consume the identifier. |
| `spec.payload.slices` | array of object |  |  |  | Slices are the disjoint slices of a sliced Workload. The integrate task unions their branches; the reconcile task checks pins against their files. Set on integrate and reconcile tasks. Part of #1033. |
| `spec.payload.slices[].branch` | string |  |  |  | Branch is the git ref the slice's coder pushed. Required for the integrate task, which applies each slice branch's diff onto the base. |
| `spec.payload.slices[].files` | array of string |  |  |  | Files are the repo-relative paths this slice owns, so a reconcile task looks for each pinned identifier in the right slice. |
| `spec.payload.slices[].name` | string | yes |  | min length 1 | Name is the slice's name within the plan. |
| `spec.priority` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | Priority is a hint for the scheduler when many tasks are Pending. Higher values dispatch first. v0.1 is FIFO and ignores priority. |
| `spec.requiredCapability` | object |  |  |  | RequiredCapability filters which FleetNodes can serve this task. Ignored when AgentRef is set (the Agent's RequiredCapability wins). |
| `spec.requiredCapability.accelerator` | string |  | `"any"` | one of `"metal"`, `"cuda"`, `"vulkan"`, `"any"` | Accelerator selects an accelerator family. "any" matches any node. |
| `spec.requiredCapability.minContextTokens` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MinContextTokens is the minimum context window the node's installed model must support. Set to 0 to leave unconstrained. |
| `spec.requiredCapability.minRAMGB` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MinRAMGB is the minimum available RAM the node must advertise. |
| `spec.requiredCapability.nodeSelector` | map of string |  |  |  | NodeSelector is a hard pin: only FleetNodes whose labels match every key are eligible. Used for tasks that must run on a specific node (e.g. verify tasks targeting the gate runner). |
| `spec.requiredCapability.requiresModelInstalled` | boolean |  |  |  | RequiresModelInstalled, when true, scopes scheduling to FleetNodes that already have this Agent's model resident (the model name, resolved from Agent.spec.model or Agent.spec.inferenceServiceRef, must appear in the node's status.capability.installedModels). In that mode the minRAMGB gate is intentionally ignored: the model is already loaded, so the agent loop needs ~0 additional RAM. This is the warm-driver path reviewer-class Agents always take; minRAMGB is correct only for the cold-load path. When false (default), minRAMGB is enforced as before. |
| `spec.requiredCapability.roles` | array of string |  |  |  | Roles filters to FleetNodes whose spec.roles include every named role. Matches against FleetNodeSpec.Roles (the `--roles` flag on the foreman-agent). Used to route the gate Agent's tasks to nodes that advertise themselves as verifiers without coupling to per-node labels. |
| `spec.scanGate` | object |  |  |  | ScanGate declares the reproducible container-image scan gate for this task (CVE/release-image gates). Unset means no scan gate — the task's post-push flow is unchanged. Consumed by the scan-gate executor. |
| `spec.scanGate.builderImage` | string |  |  |  | BuilderImage runs the Go build of each target binary. Empty defaults to the built-in builder image ("golang:1.26"). |
| `spec.scanGate.ignoreUnfixed` | boolean |  |  |  | IgnoreUnfixed mirrors Trivy's `--ignore-unfixed`. nil defaults to true (CI's setting: only findings with an available fix block). |
| `spec.scanGate.images` | array of string |  |  |  | Images lists the built-in scan-target ids to scan (e.g. "controller", "foreman-agent"). Empty means all built-in targets: a nil/empty Images is the nil-means-all rule, and Resolve passes it through as a nil ResolvedScan.Images. Unknown ids are rejected by Resolve's caller; Resolve itself ignores the list beyond the empty-means-all rule and leaves per-id validation to the tool. |
| `spec.scanGate.runnerImage` | string |  |  |  | RunnerImage assembles and scans each image (daemonless buildah + pinned trivy). Empty defaults to the built-in runner ("alpine:3.24"). |
| `spec.scanGate.severity` | array of string |  |  |  | Severity is the Trivy severity floor. Empty defaults to `{"CRITICAL","HIGH"}` (CI's floor). |
| `spec.timeoutSeconds` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | TimeoutSeconds bounds the agent's run time. Zero uses the operator's default (2700, matching the autofix pipeline's value). |
| `spec.verdictPolicy` | object |  |  |  | VerdictPolicy is the reconciler-propagated effective Workload work-class self-certification policy (proposal 1075, section 3.2) that reaches the executor without a live Workload GET. A nil policy, or one with an empty SelfGO, resolves to VerdictPolicy.Resolve's default. The WorkloadReconciler stamps this from Workload.spec.verdictPolicy onto every child task it emits, the same way it propagates MCPEnabled; hand-authored AgenticTasks may set it directly. |
| `spec.verdictPolicy.selfGO` | array of string |  |  |  | SelfGO lists work classes allowed to self-certify GO. Classes: code-fix, docs, packaging, config, ci-policy, release-policy. Default (nil): \[code-fix, docs, packaging, config\]; ci-policy and release-policy always require human sign-off unless listed here. |

#### AgenticTask status

status reflects the observed state, updated by the scheduler and the assigned FleetAgent.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.assignedNode` | string |  |  |  | AssignedNode is the FleetNode.metadata.name the scheduler routed to. |
| `status.attempt` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | Attempt counts execution claims for this task UID. It survives claim recovery so a redispatch cannot overwrite the previous run's records. |
| `status.branch` | string |  |  |  | Branch is the pushed branch, set on a successful issue-fix. |
| `status.claimedAt` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | ClaimedAt is when the FleetAgent on AssignedNode claimed the task. |
| `status.commitSHA` | string |  |  |  | CommitSHA is the head commit of Branch. |
