| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | Conditions represent the current state of the task. Standard types: Scheduled, Running, Completed, Failed. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.failureReason` | string |  |  | one of `"AgentNotFound"`, `"InferenceServiceUnavailable"`, `"AuthUnavailable"`, `"GitRemoteNotConfigured"`, `"CloneFailed"`, `"RebaseConflictUnresolved"`, `"ModelMisunderstood"`, `"ToolFailed"`, `"MaxTurnsExhausted"`, `"LoopSpinning"`, `"ConstraintViolated"`, `"Timeout"`, `"InfrastructureError"`, `"GateFailed"`, `"GateError"`, `"ModelReportedError"` | FailureReason categorizes WHY a task did not reach a "succeeded on target" outcome. Distinct from Verdict (which carries the externally-meaningful WHAT). Empty on a successful task. See AgenticTaskFailureReason for the full enum + per-reason semantics. |
| `status.finishedAt` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | FinishedAt is when the executor produced a verdict (success or fail). |
| `status.jobName` | string |  |  |  | JobName is the name of the Kubernetes Job currently running this task, set at dispatch for Job-mode Agents so operators and downstream tooling can identify the live Job while it runs (not just after completion) (#1535). |
| `status.phase` | string |  |  | one of `"Pending"`, `"Scheduled"`, `"Running"`, `"Verifying"`, `"Succeeded"`, `"Failed"` | Phase is the current lifecycle phase. |
| `status.result` | object (any fields) |  |  |  | Result is the structured JSON the agent emitted, validated against the foreman.v1 schema. Opaque to the API server. |
| `status.startedAt` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | StartedAt is when the executor began work. |
| `status.transcriptRef` | string |  |  |  | TranscriptRef points to where the agent's full transcript was stored (typically a ConfigMap in the operator's namespace). |
| `status.verdict` | string |  |  | one of `"GO"`, `"NO-GO"`, `"INCOMPLETE"`, `"GATE-PASS"`, `"GATE-FAIL"`, `"GATE-ERROR"`, `"Skipped"` | Verdict is the final outcome category. |

### FleetNode

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `FleetNode` |
| API versions | `foreman.llmkube.dev/v1alpha1` |
| Scope | Cluster |
| Plural | `fleetnodes` |
| Short names | `fn` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_fleetnodes.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_fleetnodes.yaml) |

#### FleetNode printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Phase | `string` | `.status.phase` | 0 |  |
| Version | `string` | `.status.agentVersion` | 0 |  |
| Accelerator | `string` | `.status.capability.accelerator` | 0 |  |
| RAM | `integer` | `.status.capability.availableRAMGB` | 0 |  |
| Current Task | `string` | `.status.currentTask` | 0 |  |
| Supervising | `integer` | `.status.supervisionCapacity.current` | 0 |  |
| Supervision | `integer` | `.status.supervisionCapacity.maximum` | 0 |  |
| K8s Node | `string` | `.status.kubernetesNode` | 0 |  |
| Heartbeat | `date` | `.status.lastHeartbeatTime` | 0 |  |
| Age | `date` | `.metadata.creationTimestamp` | 0 |  |

#### FleetNode spec

spec is the small bit of identity the agent owns.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.nodeName` | string | yes |  |  | NodeName is the human-readable identity of the WORKER — the agent process, not the machine it runs on (#1640). Conventionally matches metadata.name; required for the scheduler to address it. For an in-cluster agent this is the pod name (the agent falls back to os.Hostname()); off-cluster agents set it via `--fleet-node-name`. The Kubernetes node a pod is scheduled on is reported separately at status.kubernetesNode. |
| `spec.roles` | array of string |  |  |  | Roles label the node for capability-aware scheduling beyond raw accelerator type. Conventionally one or more of: "worker", "verifier". |
| `spec.tailscaleAddr` | string |  |  |  | TailscaleAddr is the Tailscale address (IP or MagicDNS name) the FleetAgent listens on. Optional; the operator does not connect to the agent directly in v0.1 (dispatch is via the agent's CRD watch). |

#### FleetNode status

status is the agent's heartbeat-driven view of its host. The scheduler reads it; the agent writes it.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.agentKind` | string |  |  | one of `"foreman-agent"`, `"metal-agent"` | AgentKind identifies which agent binary manages this FleetNode. metal-agent does not register a FleetNode in v0.1, but the enum documents intent for future use. |
| `status.agentVersion` | string |  |  |  | AgentVersion is the observed running version of the agent, reported on heartbeat. Set by the foreman-agent using the binary's build-time version string; empty for agents that predate this field. |
| `status.arch` | string |  |  |  | Arch is the CPU architecture reported by the agent on heartbeat (runtime.GOARCH). Used alongside OS for platform artifact selection. |
| `status.capability` | object |  |  |  | Capability is what this node advertises to the scheduler. |
| `status.capability.accelerator` | string |  |  | one of `"metal"`, `"cuda"`, `"vulkan"`, `"none"` | Accelerator names the accelerator family available on this node. |
| `status.capability.availableRAMGB` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | AvailableRAMGB is the FleetAgent's estimate of RAM not currently committed to a running model or task. Refreshed on heartbeat. |
| `status.capability.installedModels` | array of string |  |  |  | InstalledModels is the list of Model CR names this node has locally available (the model files are present and the runtime can load them). |
| `status.capability.maxContextTokens` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MaxContextTokens is the largest context window the loaded model supports. Used by the scheduler to filter tasks with high MinContextTokens requirements. |
| `status.capability.tokensPerSecond` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | TokensPerSecond is a coarse decode throughput estimate. v0.1 takes this from configuration; v0.2 will benchmark on heartbeat. |
| `status.capability.totalRAMGB` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | TotalRAMGB is the physical RAM in GiB. |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | Conditions track standard health signals: Ready, Draining, etc. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.currentTask` | string |  |  |  | CurrentTask is the namespaced name of the in-process AgenticTask this node is reserved for, or empty if no reservation is held. The scheduler skips nodes with a non-empty CurrentTask when placing in-process work. |
| `status.kubernetesNode` | string |  |  |  | KubernetesNode is the Kubernetes node the agent pod is scheduled on, reported by the agent on heartbeat from the FLEET_NODE_NAME downward-API env var (fieldRef: spec.nodeName). It is a PROPERTY of the worker, not its identity: FleetNode identity is the agent process (#1640), and spec.nodeName holds the worker's own name. Off-cluster agents (metal Macs) have no Kubernetes node and leave this empty. |
| `status.lastHeartbeatTime` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastHeartbeatTime is the most recent heartbeat the FleetAgent successfully patched. The reconciler marks the phase NotReady if this stalls (default threshold: 90 seconds). |
| `status.os` | string |  |  |  | OS is the operating system reported by the agent on heartbeat (runtime.GOOS). Used by the AgentReleaseReconciler to select the correct platform artifact for this node. |
| `status.phase` | string |  |  | one of `"Ready"`, `"Draining"`, `"NotReady"`, `"Unknown"` | Phase is the heartbeat-driven health state. The scheduler treats only Ready nodes as eligible. |
| `status.supervisionCapacity` | object |  |  |  | SupervisionCapacity is the node's Job-mode supervision budget, published by the agent on heartbeat (#1639). It lets an operator tell "the fleet is at capacity" apart from "something is broken": a node that is heartbeating normally but declining further Job-mode tasks because its supervision bound is full now reads as full from the cluster instead of only in the agent's memory. |
| `status.supervisionCapacity.current` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | Current is the number of Job-mode tasks this node is currently supervising. Reported on heartbeat; resets to zero when none are in flight. |
| `status.supervisionCapacity.maximum` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | Maximum is the node's Job-mode supervision bound (`--max-supervised-tasks`, defaulting to DefaultMaxSupervisedTasks). Zero means the bound is unset (unbounded); it is a pointer so an absent capacity renders as absent, not as "unbounded". |
| `status.updateRequest` | object |  |  |  | UpdateRequest is written by the AgentReleaseReconciler when a new agent version is ready for this node to install. The agent reads this field on each heartbeat (PR4) and performs the self-update. The reconciler clears the field once the node reports the target version and passes the health gate. |
| `status.updateRequest.sha256` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[0-9a-f]{64}$` | SHA256 is the lowercase hex-encoded SHA-256 digest of the artifact. |
| `status.updateRequest.targetVersion` | string | yes |  |  | TargetVersion is the version string the node should update to. |
| `status.updateRequest.url` | string | yes |  |  | URL is the download location for the agent binary artifact. |

### ModelProfile

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `ModelProfile` |
| API versions | `foreman.llmkube.dev/v1alpha1` |
| Scope | Cluster |
| Plural | `modelprofiles` |
| Short names | `mp` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_modelprofiles.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_modelprofiles.yaml) |

#### ModelProfile printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Reads-Restricted | `boolean` | `.spec.restrictReadsInForcingPhase` | 0 |  |
| Age | `date` | `.metadata.creationTimestamp` | 0 |  |

#### ModelProfile spec

spec is the per-model tuning.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.restrictReadsInForcingPhase` | boolean |  |  |  | RestrictReadsInForcingPhase, when true, makes the EditFreeStreak forcing phase also drop read_file from the advertised tool set (it normally drops only grep/bash). This forces a thrash-prone model that re-reads instead of editing to actually edit. Default false preserves the standard forcing-phase behavior. |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection` | object |  |  |  | StuckLoopDetection overrides the stuck-loop detector thresholds, but only for Agents that reference this profile AND do not set their own spec.stuckLoopDetection (the Agent's own block wins wholesale). Non-zero fields override the harness default; zero fields inherit it. |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection.contextHardCap` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | ContextHardCap is the approximate-token budget that force- terminates without a nudge stage. Must be &gt; ContextSoftCap when both are set; the executor disables both if soft &gt;= hard rather than producing inconsistent decisions. |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection.contextSoftCap` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | ContextSoftCap is the approximate-token budget that nudges the model once. Zero disables (paired with ContextHardCap). |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection.editFreeTurnsLimit` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0; max 200 | EditFreeTurnsLimit is the number of consecutive turns without a write_file, str_replace, or submit_result call required to fire the edit-free signal. Zero disables. |
| `spec.stuckLoopDetection.repeatedToolThreshold` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0; max 100 | RepeatedToolThreshold is the number of identical (tool_name, args) calls required to fire the duplicate-call signal. Zero disables. |
| `spec.systemPromptAddendum` | string |  |  |  | SystemPromptAddendum is appended (never substituted) to the referencing Agent's systemPrompt at dispatch, separated by a blank line. Use it to damp model-specific artifacts (e.g. an over-gating model that stalls on fully-authorized work). Empty is a no-op. |

#### ModelProfile status

status is reserved; the MVP has no reconciler.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | Conditions represent the latest available observations. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  |  | ObservedGeneration is the most recent generation observed. |

### Workload

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `Workload` |
| API versions | `foreman.llmkube.dev/v1alpha1` |
| Scope | Namespaced |
| Plural | `workloads` |
| Short names | `wl` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_workloads.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/foreman.llmkube.dev_workloads.yaml) |

#### Workload printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Phase | `string` | `.status.phase` | 0 |  |
| Repo | `string` | `.spec.repo` | 0 |  |
| Tasks | `integer` | `.status.succeededTasks` | 0 |  |
| Failed | `integer` | `.status.failedTasks` | 0 |  |
| Age | `date` | `.metadata.creationTimestamp` | 0 |  |

#### Workload spec

spec is the user-supplied intent.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.allowCloudReviewers` | boolean |  |  |  | AllowCloudReviewers gates whether reviewer Agents whose spec.provider is "cloud-proxy" (or any non-"local" value) may be dispatched for this Workload. Three-valued via the \*bool: |
| `spec.allowOverwrite` | boolean |  |  |  | AllowOverwrite lets this Workload's coder replace a stale remote ref for its own foreman/\* branch (force-with-lease compare-and-swap) instead of failing non-fast-forward. Opt-in — #573 deliberately rejected force-on-collision as a default; retry systems that re-run Workloads under the same name set this on the re-run, where the stale ref is their own previous attempt. Copied onto every synthesized issue-batch task payload. |
| `spec.coderAgentRef` | object |  |  |  | CoderAgentRef is required when Issues is set: names the same- namespace Agent the code-&lt;N&gt; steps reference. Ignored in explicit Pipeline mode. |
| `spec.coderAgentRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.escalateOnFailure` | boolean |  |  |  | EscalateOnFailure, when true, additionally re-attempts the issue on EscalationCoderAgentRef for a terminal STUCK-LOOP-DETECTED, INCOMPLETE, or ERROR from the base coder (not just a capability NO-GO). Requires EscalationCoderAgentRef to be set. Default false preserves the NO-GO-only behavior. ALREADY-RESOLVED and NEEDS-VERIFICATION never escalate. |
| `spec.escalationCoderAgentRef` | object |  |  |  | EscalationCoderAgentRef is the optional coder escalation tier: a single, typically larger/denser coder Agent that re-attempts an issue when the base CoderAgentRef fails at its model's ceiling. Issue-batch mode only. For each issue N, if the base code-&lt;N&gt; task is terminal with a capability failure (verdict NO-GO from the model, or outcome CODER-GATE-FAILED), the WorkloadReconciler emits an escalation code+verify(+review) set (code-&lt;N&gt;-esc / verify-&lt;N&gt;-esc / review-&lt;N&gt;-esc-&lt;i&gt;) against this Agent, on a fresh branch foreman/&lt;w&gt;/issue-&lt;N&gt;-esc, with the failed model's summary passed as a prompt hint. |
| `spec.escalationCoderAgentRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.escalationReviewerAgentRefs` | array of object |  |  |  | EscalationReviewerAgentRefs is the optional second reviewer tier (v0.2, #546). For each issue N in issue-batch mode, the WorkloadReconciler emits one review task per listed Agent (rendered name "&lt;workload&gt;-escalate-&lt;N&gt;-&lt;j&gt;") ONLY after every base reviewer task for that issue (the ReviewerAgentRefs fan-out) is terminal AND at least one returned verdict=NO-GO. This bounds the cost of an expensive reviewer (typically a larger local model; a cloud-proxy Agent is allowed but stays behind the same sovereignty gates as base reviewers) to the small fraction of branches a cheap reviewer already flagged. |
| `spec.escalationReviewerAgentRefs[].name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.gateProfile` | object |  |  |  | GateProfile is the default gate profile applied to every AgenticTask this Workload decomposes into (issue-batch, explicit pipeline, and escalation steps alike). It sets the verify gate's language, image, and commands, and drives the coder's in-loop self-gate. A PipelineStep may override it per step (see PipelineStep.GateProfile); an unset profile on both resolves to the "go" preset, i.e. current behavior. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands` | object |  |  |  | Commands overrides the preset commands. Only non-empty fields replace the preset; empty fields keep the preset value. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.build` | string |  |  |  | Build is the command that compiles the project. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.codegenCheck` | string |  |  |  | CodegenCheck is the command that verifies generated files are in sync with their sources. Empty means no codegen check. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.format` | string |  |  |  | Format is the command that checks source formatting. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.lint` | string |  |  |  | Lint is the command that runs the linter. |
| `spec.gateProfile.commands.test` | string |  |  |  | Test is the command that runs the test suite. |
| `spec.gateProfile.image` | string |  |  |  | Image is the container image used to run the gate checks. |
| `spec.gateProfile.language` | string |  |  | one of `"go"`, `"python"`, `"rust"`, `"node"`, `"generic"` | Language selects the built-in preset. Defaults to "go" when empty. |
| `spec.gateProfile.sourceExtensions` | array of string |  |  |  | SourceExtensions lists the file extensions the gate should consider as source files (e.g. ".go", ".py"). |
| `spec.gateProfile.testLayout` | object |  |  |  | TestLayout declares where this repository's tests live relative to the code they cover, so the reviewer's scope-overlap check can map a test file back to the module it covers. Leave unset when tests sit beside the code, which is the common case and the built-in behaviour. |
| `spec.gateProfile.testLayout.sourceRoot` | string |  |  |  | SourceRoot is the directory the code under test lives under, e.g. "src/main/java" for Maven/Gradle, or "lib" for Ruby. |
| `spec.gateProfile.testLayout.testRoot` | string |  |  |  | TestRoot is the directory tests live under, e.g. "src/test/java" for Maven/Gradle, or "spec" for a Ruby project. |
| `spec.intent` | string | yes |  | min length 1 | Intent is the natural-language description of what to do. Example: "fix all open bugs in defilantech/LLMKube tagged size/small". |
| `spec.issues` | array of integer (int32) |  |  |  | Issues, when set and Pipeline is empty, drives the issue-batch shortcut. The reconciler emits one code/verify pair per issue number using CoderAgentRef + VerifierAgentRef. Repo + Branch + Issue fields are populated from the Workload's spec.repo and the issue numbers themselves; downstream verify tasks DependOn the upstream code task automatically. |
| `spec.maxReviewIterations` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MaxReviewIterations bounds how many fix iterations the WorkloadReconciler may append per issue after a reviewer NO-GO (#946). On a NO-GO, instead of failing the Workload, the reconciler emits a new coder task ("code-&lt;N&gt;-r&lt;k&gt;", using RevisionCoderAgentRef when set and CoderAgentRef otherwise, same branch, payload.reviseFromBranch naming that branch so the executor restores the prior attempt (#951), and payload.allowOverwrite=true) whose payload.prompt carries the reviewer's structured findings and summary, then chains a fresh verify + reviewer fan-out behind it ("verify-&lt;N&gt;-r&lt;k&gt;", "review-&lt;N&gt;-&lt;i&gt;-r&lt;k&gt;"). Superseded iterations stop counting toward the rollup; the Workload completes when the final iteration's reviewers say GO and fails (today's behavior) when the last allowed iteration is still NO-GO. |
| `spec.maxTasks` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | MaxTasks caps how many AgenticTasks may be emitted. Zero means no limit. Applied after pipeline expansion: if Issues has 20 entries and MaxTasks is 10, only the first 10 issues are processed and a Truncated condition is set. |
| `spec.mcpEnabled` | boolean |  |  |  | MCPEnabled is a benchmark opt-out for MCP tool access. Three-valued via the \*bool: nil or true means MCP is allowed for Agents in this Workload that have spec.mcp configured; false disables MCP for every child task regardless of the referenced Agent's own MCP config, for control runs that must be comparable to a no-MCP baseline. The WorkloadReconciler propagates this value onto each child AgenticTask.spec.mcpEnabled. |
| `spec.openPullRequest` | boolean |  |  |  | OpenPullRequest controls whether a review-GO opens a pull request for the Workload's branch (the pipeline's end artifact). Three- valued via the \*bool: nil (unset) means true in issue-batch mode — a Workload built from Issues exists to produce a PR — while \*false opts a Workload out (branch-only consumers). Explicit pipelines carry the flag per step and ignore this field. Idempotent at the executor: an existing PR for the branch is reused, never duplicated (#937). |
| `spec.pipeline` | array of object |  |  |  | Pipeline, when set, bypasses the planner and emits AgenticTasks verbatim from this list. Each step becomes one AgenticTask owner-ref'd to this Workload, named "&lt;workload-name&gt;-&lt;step.name&gt;". Pipeline takes precedence over Issues + agent-ref shortcuts when both are set. Used for hand-authored pipelines and re-runs. |
| `spec.pipeline[].agentRef` | object | yes |  |  | AgentRef names the same-namespace Agent that runs this step. |
| `spec.pipeline[].agentRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.pipeline[].dependsOn` | array of string |  |  |  | DependsOn lists other PipelineStep.Name values within this Workload whose AgenticTasks must reach Succeeded before this step's task is dispatched. The reconciler rewrites these to absolute task names when rendering. |
| `spec.pipeline[].gateProfile` | object |  |  |  | GateProfile overrides the Workload-level GateProfile for this step only, so a mixed-language pipeline can gate each step differently. Unset falls back to WorkloadSpec.GateProfile, then to the "go" preset. |
| `spec.pipeline[].gateProfile.commands` | object |  |  |  | Commands overrides the preset commands. Only non-empty fields replace the preset; empty fields keep the preset value. Has 5 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.pipeline[].gateProfile.image` | string |  |  |  | Image is the container image used to run the gate checks. |
| `spec.pipeline[].gateProfile.language` | string |  |  | one of `"go"`, `"python"`, `"rust"`, `"node"`, `"generic"` | Language selects the built-in preset. Defaults to "go" when empty. |
| `spec.pipeline[].gateProfile.sourceExtensions` | array of string |  |  |  | SourceExtensions lists the file extensions the gate should consider as source files (e.g. ".go", ".py"). |
| `spec.pipeline[].gateProfile.testLayout` | object |  |  |  | TestLayout declares where this repository's tests live relative to the code they cover, so the reviewer's scope-overlap check can map a test file back to the module it covers. Leave unset when tests sit beside the code, which is the common case and the built-in behaviour. Has 2 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.pipeline[].kind` | string | yes |  | one of `"issue-fix"`, `"verify"`, `"review"`, `"freeform"`, `"integrate"`, `"reconcile"` | Kind selects the AgenticTask kind to emit. |
| `spec.pipeline[].name` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$`; min length 1 | Name identifies the step within this Workload's pipeline. Becomes the suffix of the rendered AgenticTask name and the target ID for other steps' DependsOn entries. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload` | object | yes |  |  | Payload mirrors AgenticTaskSpec.Payload exactly. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.agent` | string |  |  |  | Agent is the named agent to invoke. Required for freeform; defaults to "issue-fixer" for issue-fix and "verify" for verify. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.allowOverwrite` | boolean |  |  |  | AllowOverwrite permits the coder to replace a stale remote ref for its own branch (force-with-lease pinned to the observed SHA) when a plain push is rejected non-fast-forward. Opt-in, per the design decision in #573: silently overwriting published branches is the wrong default for a system that leaves an audit trail, but automated retry systems that delete-and-recreate Workloads derive the same branch name every run and are otherwise permanently wedged by their predecessor's ref (#934). Stamped from Workload.spec.allowOverwrite in issue-batch mode; explicit pipelines set it per step. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.baseBranch` | string |  |  |  | BaseBranch is the upstream base ref the coder branch is cut from (and the verify gate's bite check diffs against). On an issue-fix task the executor fetches this ref from the upstream repo (payload.repo) and branches off it, so a stale fork default branch does not produce a stale-base branch. Optional; defaults to "main". |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.branch` | string |  |  |  | Branch is the existing branch to gate. Required for verify. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.branchPrefix` | string |  |  |  | BranchPrefix overrides the branch name prefix on issue-fix tasks (default derived from the issue's labels via conventional commit prefixes: fix/, feat/, chore/, etc.). |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.branchStrategy` | string |  | `"reset"` | one of `"reset"`, `"rebase"` | BranchStrategy selects how the working branch is cut relative to the current base (see BranchStrategy). Defaults to "reset": cut fresh from the current base and ignore any prior attempt (reviseFromBranch), so a retry or repair re-dispatch cannot revert merged work. Set "rebase" for the in-review PR revision path, where the prior attempt is restored and rebased onto the current base. Issue-fix only. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.contract` | string |  |  |  | Contract is the slice plan's prose contract, handed to a reconcile task's LLM sweep as the description of the intended shared interfaces. Reconcile only. Part of #1033. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.gateAdvisories` | array of object |  |  |  | GateAdvisories holds non-blocking findings copied from the upstream coder task's result, surfaced to a reviewer for confirmation or dismissal. The WorkloadReconciler populates this field on review-kind tasks once the coder task has a terminal result with advisories in status.result.extra\["gateAdvisories"\]. Empty when the coder task produced no advisories or has not yet completed. Has 2 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.images` | array of string |  |  |  | Images are workspace-relative paths to images attached to the first user message, for agents whose model can accept them (Agent.spec.vision.enabled). The intended use is showing a coder what its own change looks like: a screenshot of a rendered page alongside the prompt describing what is wrong with it. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.issue` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 1 | Issue is the GitHub issue number. Required for issue-fix. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.openPullRequest` | boolean |  |  |  | OpenPullRequest instructs the reviewer-role executor to open a pull request for the task's branch when its review verdict is GO (idempotent: an existing PR for the branch is reused). Stamped from Workload.spec.openPullRequest onto review steps in issue-batch mode; explicit pipelines set it per step. The PR is the Workload's artifact — without this the pipeline ends at a reviewed-but-unopened branch (#937). |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.prompt` | string |  |  |  | Prompt is the agent input. Required for freeform. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.promptPrefix` | string |  |  |  | PromptPrefix is prepended to the assembled user prompt for an issue-fix task, BEFORE the fetched issue body. Unlike Prompt (which, when set, suppresses the issue-body fetch), PromptPrefix leaves the issue-body fetch intact, so the agent sees both this hint and the issue's acceptance criteria. Used by the coder escalation tier to carry the prior model's diagnosis. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.repo` | string |  |  |  | Repo is the "owner/name" GitHub repo. Required for issue-fix and verify. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.reviseFromBranch` | string |  |  |  | ReviseFromBranch names a ref on the push remote that the executor cuts the working branch FROM instead of from BaseBranch, so a revision task starts with its prior attempt's files present (#951). Issue-fix only. The WorkloadReconciler stamps this on fix- iteration coder tasks (the prior attempt lives at the task's own branch name on the push remote). The executor owns the git restore — no prompt-driven fetch/checkout. If the ref does not exist on the remote (e.g. pruned, or the prior attempt never pushed), the executor logs and falls back to branching from BaseBranch rather than failing. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.sharedIdentifiers` | array of object |  |  |  | SharedIdentifiers pins the exact cross-slice strings a reconcile task verifies against the integrated union. Reconcile only. Part of #1033. Has 3 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.pipeline[].payload.slices` | array of object |  |  |  | Slices are the disjoint slices of a sliced Workload. The integrate task unions their branches; the reconcile task checks pins against their files. Set on integrate and reconcile tasks. Part of #1033. Has 3 nested fields; see the YAML. |
| `spec.pipeline[].priority` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | Priority is a scheduler hint when multiple tasks are Pending. Higher values dispatch first. v0.1 is FIFO and ignores priority. |
| `spec.pipeline[].scanGate` | object |  |  |  | ScanGate overrides the Workload-level ScanGate for this step only. Unset falls back to WorkloadSpec.ScanGate, then to no scan gate. |
| `spec.pipeline[].scanGate.builderImage` | string |  |  |  | BuilderImage runs the Go build of each target binary. Empty defaults to the built-in builder image ("golang:1.26"). |
| `spec.pipeline[].scanGate.ignoreUnfixed` | boolean |  |  |  | IgnoreUnfixed mirrors Trivy's `--ignore-unfixed`. nil defaults to true (CI's setting: only findings with an available fix block). |
| `spec.pipeline[].scanGate.images` | array of string |  |  |  | Images lists the built-in scan-target ids to scan (e.g. "controller", "foreman-agent"). Empty means all built-in targets: a nil/empty Images is the nil-means-all rule, and Resolve passes it through as a nil ResolvedScan.Images. Unknown ids are rejected by Resolve's caller; Resolve itself ignores the list beyond the empty-means-all rule and leaves per-id validation to the tool. |
| `spec.pipeline[].scanGate.runnerImage` | string |  |  |  | RunnerImage assembles and scans each image (daemonless buildah + pinned trivy). Empty defaults to the built-in runner ("alpine:3.24"). |
| `spec.pipeline[].scanGate.severity` | array of string |  |  |  | Severity is the Trivy severity floor. Empty defaults to `{"CRITICAL","HIGH"}` (CI's floor). |
| `spec.pipeline[].timeoutSeconds` | integer (int32) |  |  | min 0 | TimeoutSeconds bounds the agent's run time on this step. Zero uses the operator's default (2700 seconds). |
| `spec.plannerModel` | string |  |  |  | PlannerModel selects the frontier model the planner should call. Empty uses the operator's default (Anthropic Claude). v0.1 stores this verbatim but does not consume it (planner is deferred to v0.2); the value round-trips through status.plannerModel for visibility. |
| `spec.repo` | string |  |  |  | Repo is the GitHub repo in "owner/name" form that Intent applies to. Required for issue-fix workloads; supplies the Payload.Repo for each generated AgenticTask in issue-batch mode. Ignored in explicit Pipeline mode (each step carries its own Payload.Repo). |
| `spec.reviewerAgentRefs` | array of object |  |  |  | ReviewerAgentRefs is the optional v0.2 third pipeline stage: each listed Agent runs a review task against the diff the coder produced, in parallel, all depending on the upstream verify task reaching GATE-PASS. The rendered task names are "&lt;workload&gt;-review-&lt;N&gt;-&lt;i&gt;" where N is the issue number and i is the index into this slice; the index keeps DNS-1123 lengths bounded across long agent names. |
| `spec.reviewerAgentRefs[].name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.revisionCoderAgentRef` | object |  |  |  | RevisionCoderAgentRef optionally names the same-namespace Agent the fix-iteration coder steps ("code-&lt;N&gt;-r&lt;k&gt;") reference instead of CoderAgentRef. A revision task amends its restored prior attempt (payload.reviseFromBranch, #951) rather than building a fix from scratch, and the issue-fix Agent's forcing profile is tuned for the latter — #951's live validation showed a revision task collapsing under the issue-fix profile's forcing windows. When unset, iteration coder steps fall back to CoderAgentRef and the reconciler emits a Warning event on the Workload (reason RevisionUnderIssueFixProfile). Issue-batch mode only; ignored in explicit Pipeline mode. |
| `spec.revisionCoderAgentRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `spec.scanGate` | object |  |  |  | ScanGate is the default container-image scan gate applied to every AgenticTask this Workload decomposes into (issue-batch, explicit pipeline, and escalation steps alike). A PipelineStep may override it per step (see PipelineStep.ScanGate); unset on both means no scan gate — the behavior before Workloads carried one. |
| `spec.scanGate.builderImage` | string |  |  |  | BuilderImage runs the Go build of each target binary. Empty defaults to the built-in builder image ("golang:1.26"). |
| `spec.scanGate.ignoreUnfixed` | boolean |  |  |  | IgnoreUnfixed mirrors Trivy's `--ignore-unfixed`. nil defaults to true (CI's setting: only findings with an available fix block). |
| `spec.scanGate.images` | array of string |  |  |  | Images lists the built-in scan-target ids to scan (e.g. "controller", "foreman-agent"). Empty means all built-in targets: a nil/empty Images is the nil-means-all rule, and Resolve passes it through as a nil ResolvedScan.Images. Unknown ids are rejected by Resolve's caller; Resolve itself ignores the list beyond the empty-means-all rule and leaves per-id validation to the tool. |
| `spec.scanGate.runnerImage` | string |  |  |  | RunnerImage assembles and scans each image (daemonless buildah + pinned trivy). Empty defaults to the built-in runner ("alpine:3.24"). |
| `spec.scanGate.severity` | array of string |  |  |  | Severity is the Trivy severity floor. Empty defaults to `{"CRITICAL","HIGH"}` (CI's floor). |
| `spec.verdictPolicy` | object |  |  |  | VerdictPolicy controls which work classes a coder GO may self-certify for every AgenticTask this Workload decomposes into (proposal 1075, section 3.2). Unset resolves to VerdictPolicy.Resolve's default (code-fix, docs, packaging, config); ci-policy and release-policy stay human-sign-off-only unless an operator opts them in here. The WorkloadReconciler propagates this value onto each child AgenticTask.spec.verdictPolicy the same way it propagates MCPEnabled, so the executor reads the policy without a live Workload GET. |
| `spec.verdictPolicy.selfGO` | array of string |  |  |  | SelfGO lists work classes allowed to self-certify GO. Classes: code-fix, docs, packaging, config, ci-policy, release-policy. Default (nil): \[code-fix, docs, packaging, config\]; ci-policy and release-policy always require human sign-off unless listed here. |
| `spec.verifierAgentRef` | object |  |  |  | VerifierAgentRef names the same-namespace Agent the verify-&lt;N&gt; steps reference. Optional in the issue-batch shortcut: when unset, no deterministic verify gate is emitted and each issue's pipeline is code -&gt; review (reviews depend directly on the code step); verification then rests on the coder's own test run and the repository's CI on the opened PR. Ignored in explicit Pipeline mode. |
| `spec.verifierAgentRef.name` | string |  | `""` |  | Name of the referent. This field is effectively required, but due to backwards compatibility is allowed to be empty. Instances of this type with an empty value here are almost certainly wrong. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |

#### Workload status

status is the planner's and scheduler's observed view.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.conditions` | array of object |  |  |  | Conditions track standard signals: Planned, Dispatched, Completed. |
| `status.conditions[].lastTransitionTime` | string (date-time) | yes |  |  | lastTransitionTime is the last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This should be when the underlying condition changed. If that is not known, then using the time when the API field changed is acceptable. |
| `status.conditions[].message` | string | yes |  | max length 32768 | message is a human readable message indicating details about the transition. This may be an empty string. |
| `status.conditions[].observedGeneration` | integer (int64) |  |  | min 0 | observedGeneration represents the .metadata.generation that the condition was set based upon. For instance, if .metadata.generation is currently 12, but the .status.conditions\[x\].observedGeneration is 9, the condition is out of date with respect to the current state of the instance. |
| `status.conditions[].reason` | string | yes |  | pattern `^[A-Za-z]([A-Za-z0-9_,:]*[A-Za-z0-9_])?$`; min length 1; max length 1024 | reason contains a programmatic identifier indicating the reason for the condition's last transition. Producers of specific condition types may define expected values and meanings for this field, and whether the values are considered a guaranteed API. The value should be a CamelCase string. This field may not be empty. |
| `status.conditions[].status` | string | yes |  | one of `"True"`, `"False"`, `"Unknown"` | status of the condition, one of True, False, Unknown. |
| `status.conditions[].type` | string | yes |  | pattern `^([a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]*[a-z0-9])?)*/)?(([A-Za-z0-9][-A-Za-z0-9_.]*)?[A-Za-z0-9])$`; max length 316 | type of condition in CamelCase or in foo.example.com/CamelCase. |
| `status.contradictedTasks` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | ContradictedTasks counts child tasks whose terminal result carried a cross-stage contradiction (extra.crossStageContradictions non-empty). A contradiction means a stage (coder / gate / reviewer) asserted something the ground-truth branch facts contradict (e.g. the coder claims it edited files on an empty branch). It is surfaced here so a contradiction is countable and visible instead of being written to the AgenticTask and read by nobody (#1685). Orthogonal to success: a task can Succeeded and still contradict itself, so it is counted in addition to whatever terminal bucket it already matched and does not move the Workload to Failed. |
| `status.failedTasks` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | FailedTasks counts child tasks in phase Failed. |
| `status.incompleteTasks` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | IncompleteTasks counts child tasks that reached Phase=Succeeded but did not produce a usable artifact (verdict in `{INCOMPLETE, NO-GO, GATE-FAIL, GATE-ERROR}`). These are surfaced separately from FailedTasks so callers can distinguish "the runtime errored" from "the agent legitimately gave up or the gate Job said the branch didn't pass the checks." |
| `status.phase` | string |  |  | one of `"Planning"`, `"Planned"`, `"Dispatched"`, `"Completed"`, `"Failed"` | Phase is the lifecycle state. |
| `status.plannerModel` | string |  |  |  | PlannerModel records which frontier model the planner actually used for this workload. Set after the planner runs. |
| `status.reviewIterations` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | ReviewIterations counts the fix iterations the reconciler has emitted after reviewer NO-GO verdicts (#946), summed across issues. Zero (or absent) means no reviewer ever bounced a branch back to the coder; operators watch this to distinguish convergence (small counts) from thrash (counts near issues \* maxReviewIterations). |
| `status.succeededTasks` | integer (int32) |  |  |  | SucceededTasks counts child tasks that reached a positive terminal outcome (Phase=Succeeded AND verdict in `{GO, GATE-PASS}`). A child in Phase=Succeeded with verdict INCOMPLETE / NO-GO / GATE-FAIL / GATE-ERROR is NOT counted here; it lands in IncompleteTasks. |
| `status.tasks` | array of object |  |  |  | Tasks lists the AgenticTask objects the planner emitted. They are owner-ref'd to this Workload so they cascade-delete with it. |
| `status.tasks[].apiVersion` | string |  |  |  | API version of the referent. |
| `status.tasks[].fieldPath` | string |  |  |  | If referring to a piece of an object instead of an entire object, this string should contain a valid JSON/Go field access statement, such as desiredState.manifest.containers\[2\]. For example, if the object reference is to a container within a pod, this would take on a value like: "`spec.containers{name}`" (where "name" refers to the name of the container that triggered the event) or if no container name is specified "spec.containers\[2\]" (container with index 2 in this pod). This syntax is chosen only to have some well-defined way of referencing a part of an object. |
| `status.tasks[].kind` | string |  |  |  | Kind of the referent. More info: `https://git.k8s.io/community/contributors/devel/sig-architecture/api-conventions.md#types-kinds` |
| `status.tasks[].name` | string |  |  |  | Name of the referent. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#names` |
| `status.tasks[].namespace` | string |  |  |  | Namespace of the referent. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/namespaces/` |
| `status.tasks[].resourceVersion` | string |  |  |  | Specific resourceVersion to which this reference is made, if any. More info: `https://git.k8s.io/community/contributors/devel/sig-architecture/api-conventions.md#concurrency-control-and-consistency` |
| `status.tasks[].uid` | string |  |  |  | UID of the referent. More info: `https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/names/#uids` |

## federation.llmkube.dev

### FederatedCluster

| Property | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | `FederatedCluster` |
| API versions | `federation.llmkube.dev/v1alpha1` |
| Scope | Cluster |
| Plural | `federatedclusters` |
| Short names | `fedcluster`, `fc` |
| Schema | [`config/crd/bases/federation.llmkube.dev_federatedclusters.yaml`](https://github.com/defilantech/LLMKube/blob/main/config/crd/bases/federation.llmkube.dev_federatedclusters.yaml) |

#### FederatedCluster printer columns

Priority 0 columns show in `kubectl get`; the others only with `-o wide`.

| Name | Type | JSONPath | Priority | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tier | `string` | `.spec.dataResidencyTier` | 0 |  |
| Phase | `string` | `.status.phase` | 0 |  |
| Last Heartbeat | `date` | `.status.lastHeartbeatTime` | 0 |  |
| GPUs | `string` | `.status.capacity.gpusAllocatable` | 0 |  |

#### FederatedCluster spec

FederatedClusterSpec is admin-owned. displayName and dataResidencyTier are set at registration; the edge and the datacenter controller only write status.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `spec.dataResidencyTier` | string |  |  |  | DataResidencyTier is recorded now and enforced later by the federation router (#1237). It is a free-form tier label (for example "eu", "floor-3"). |
| `spec.displayName` | string |  |  |  | DisplayName is a human label for the site. |
| `spec.heartbeatIntervalSeconds` | integer (int32) |  | `30` | min 5 | HeartbeatIntervalSeconds is how often the edge is expected to push status. The datacenter derives staleness thresholds from it (3x Stale, 10x Unreachable). The schema accepts intervals below the edge's fixed 30s push cadence; the hub clamps them up to that cadence before deriving thresholds, so a healthy site is never read Stale on a cadence the edge cannot deliver. |

#### FederatedCluster status

FederatedClusterStatus has two writers. The edge writes everything except Phase, over an RBAC-scoped status-subresource client. The datacenter controller writes ONLY Phase, from LastHeartbeatTime staleness.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Validation | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status.capacity` | object |  |  |  | Capacity is edge-owned node/GPU capacity. |
| `status.capacity.gpusAllocatable` | integer (int32) | yes |  |  |  |
| `status.capacity.gpusTotal` | integer (int32) | yes |  |  |  |
| `status.capacity.nodes` | integer (int32) | yes |  |  |  |
| `status.inference` | object |  |  |  | Inference is edge-owned inference health. |
| `status.inference.models` | integer (int32) | yes |  |  |  |
| `status.inference.servicesFailed` | integer (int32) | yes |  |  |  |
| `status.inference.servicesReady` | integer (int32) | yes |  |  |  |
| `status.inference.servicesTotal` | integer (int32) | yes |  |  |  |
| `status.lastHeartbeatTime` | string (date-time) |  |  |  | LastHeartbeatTime is edge-owned: set to now on every successful push. |
| `status.observedVersion` | string |  |  |  | ObservedVersion is edge-owned: the LLMKube/operator version on the site. |
| `status.phase` | string |  |  |  | Phase is datacenter-owned: Connected, Stale, or Unreachable. |
